<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Patterson's first-moment conjecture for normalized cubic Gauss sums over all primary Eisenstein primes, unconditionally. The sharp-cutoff main term is $`(6/5)c_*X^{5/6}/\log X`$, where $`c_*=(2\pi)^{2/3}/(3\Gamma(2/3))`$. For every fixed nonzero angular Fourier mode of the prime argument, we also prove cancellation at the first-moment scale.

- **claim-primary-prime-first-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): As $X\longrightarrow\infty$, $$\label{eq:main} \sum_{\substack{N\pi\le X\\\pi\equiv1\pmod3\ \mathrm{prime}}}G(\pi) =\frac65c_*\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X} +o\!\left(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\right).$$ The sum is over all primary primes in $\mathcal O$.
  - Explanation: Establishes the unconditional sharp-cutoff first moment over all primary Eisenstein primes, with the stated positive main term and a little-oh remainder.
- **claim-cubic-large-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-cls)): Let $A,B\ge1$, and let $(u_b)$ be supported on primary squarefree elements with $Nb\asymp B$. Then, for every $\epsilon>0$, $$\sum_{Na\asymp A}\mu^2(a) \left\|\sum_b u_b\chi_a(b)\right\|^2 \ll_\epsilon (AB)^\epsilon \bigl(A+B+(AB)^{2/3}\bigr)\sum_b\|u_b\|^2.$$ The same assertion holds with upper cutoffs in place of dyads.
  - Explanation: Bounds the character second moment with squarefree support in both arguments and also permits upper cutoffs.
- **claim-ordinary-character-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve)): For distinct primitive ray characters of trivial infinite parameter, with conductor norm at most $Q$ and with conductor above $3$ dividing a fixed modulus, and coefficients supported on a norm dyad of length $Z$, one has $$\sum_\chi\left\|\sum_n v_n\chi(n)\right\|^2 \ll (Z+Q^2)\sum_n\|v_n\|^2.$$ The implied constant is uniform when a fixed additional modulus above $3$ is allowed. In particular, the bound applies to the cubic characters indexed by coprime primary squarefree $p,q$ through $b\mapsto\chi_b(pq^2)$, whose conductor away from $3$ is $pq$. There is no $Q^\epsilon$ loss.
  - Explanation: Provides the ordinary character sieve without a small-power conductor loss, including the specified cubic character family.
- **claim-nonsquarefree-sieve-extensions** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cubic-extensions)): The following estimates allow an arbitrarily small $X^\epsilon$ loss. First, for divisor-bounded coefficients $(v_n)$ of norm length $Z$, without a squarefree restriction, $$\sum_{Na\asymp P}\mu^2(a) \left\|\sum_n v_n\chi_a(n)\right\|^2 \ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon Z \bigl(P+Z+(PZ)^{2/3}\bigr). \label{eq:inner-extension}$$ Second, for coefficients $(u_b)$ on primary squarefree elements of norm length $P$, $$\sum_{Na\asymp J}\left\|\sum_b u_b\chi_b(a)\right\|^2 \ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon \bigl(J+(JP)^{2/3}+J^{1/3}P\bigr)\sum_b\|u_b\|^2. \label{eq:outer-extension}$$ The latter also holds when $a$ ranges over all nonzero elements of $\mathcal O$ of norm $\asymp J$. If $J\gg P$, its last term is absorbed by $(JP)^{2/3}$.
  - Explanation: Extends the cubic sieve separately to unrestricted divisor-bounded inner coefficients and unrestricted outer arguments; these extensions retain their distinct bounds.
- **claim-level-voronoi** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-voronoi-background)): Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, and let $W$ be smooth and compactly supported in $(0,\infty)$. Put $R=Nr$ and $A_0=(2\pi)^{5/3}/(3^{7/2}\Gamma(2/3))$. The completed sum $$\mathcal C_r(V;W)= \sum_{\substack{d,u\\(d,r)=1}}\|d\|G(ur) W\left(\frac{N(ud^3)}{V}\right)$$ has main term $A_0\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\varphi(r)R^{-7/6}$. Its remaining term is $$\frac{R^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\pi)^2} \sum_{\substack{0\ne\nu\in\lambda^{-1}\mathcal O, d\\(d,r)=1}} \frac{a_r(\nu)b_r(\nu)}{N\nu\,(Nd)^{5/2}} \check W\left(\frac{(2\pi)^4N(d^3\nu)V}{R^2}\right),$$ where, for sufficiently small $\sigma>0$, $$\check W(v)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(-\sigma)}v^z \frac{\Gamma(5/6-z)\Gamma(7/6-z)} {\Gamma(z-1/6)\Gamma(z+1/6)}\mathcal MW(z)\,dz.$$ The coefficients depend only on $r$ and $\nu$, independently of $V$ and $W$. They vanish outside representations $$\nu=\lambda^j\zeta h w(h')^3,\quad j\ge-1,\quad h,h'\mid r^\infty,\quad (w,r)=1,\quad \mu^2(hw)=1,$$ where $h,h',w$ are primary and $\zeta$ is a unit. On this support, they are $$a_r(\nu)=a^\star(\lambda^{-3}\nu),\qquad b_r(\nu)=\mu\left(\frac{r}{(\lambda\nu,r)}\right) \frac{\varphi(r)}{\varphi\left(r/(\lambda\nu,r)\right)},$$ where $a^\star$ is the theta coefficient in [[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (5.74)]; the second formula is its Equation (5.68). The index $\lambda^{-3}\nu$ is the one supplied by its coefficient identity (5.79). They satisfy $$\|a_r(\nu)\|\ll 3^{\max(j,0)/3}\|h'\|, \qquad \|b_r(\nu)\|\le N((\lambda\nu,r)).$$ In particular, if $V,V^{-1},R\ll X^{C_0}$ for fixed $C_0$, $W$ is supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, and each fixed derivative bound is at most a fixed logarithmic power, then $$\mathcal C_r(V;W)= A_0\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\frac{\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}} +O_\epsilon(X^\epsilon R^{1/2}). \label{eq:voronoi}$$
  - Explanation: States the unconditional completed level summation formula, its dual coefficient support and bounds, and the resulting remainder under polynomial scale and logarithmic smoothness restrictions.
- **claim-metaplectic-height-mean-square** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-hb-height)): For primary squarefree $r$, the Dirichlet series $$f_r(s)=\sum_u G(ru)(Nu)^{-s},\qquad \operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ continues meromorphically to $\operatorname{Re}s>1/2$, with at most a simple pole at $5/6$. It has polynomial growth in closed strips there, away from its pole. For sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$ and every $T\ge1$, $$\int_{-T}^T\|f_r(1/2+\epsilon+it)\|^2dt \ll_\epsilon (Nr)^{1/2+4\epsilon}T^2. \label{eq:hb-mean-square}$$ Its residue $\rho_r$ satisfies $\|\rho_r\|\ll (Nr)^{-1/6}$.
  - Explanation: Supplies meromorphic continuation, strip growth, a height mean-square bound and residue control for squarefree levels.
- **claim-type-i-model-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-typei)): Suppose $RU\asymp X$ and $1\le R\ll X^{.51}$. Let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded and supported on $Nr\asymp R$. Let $W_r$ have support in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $L$, uniformly in $r$. Then $$\sum_r a_r\sum_u \left(G(ru)-c_*\frac{\mu^2(ru)}{N(ru)^{1/6}}\right)W_r(Nu/U) \ll X^{5/6-\eta}$$ for some fixed $\eta>0$. One may take $\eta=1/100$, with constants depending on the stated fixed parameters.
  - Explanation: Compares the Gauss-sum kernel with its squarefree model for divisor-bounded outer coefficients and logarithmically smooth inner weights, with the stated fixed power saving.
- **claim-type-i-height-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-typeiheight)): Suppose $RU\asymp X$, and let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded on $Nr\asymp R$. Suppose the smooth compactly supported weights $W_r$ have fixed support ratios and uniformly bounded derivatives, independently of $X$. For $T\ge2$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\begin{aligned} &\int_{\|t\|\asymp T}\sum_{Nr\asymp R}\|a_r\| \left\|\sum_uG(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\right\|\frac{dt}{T} \notag\\ &\hspace{12mm}\ll_\epsilon X^{1/2+\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2} +O_D\bigl(X^{5/6}L^{C}T^{-D}\bigr), \label{eq:typeI-height} \end{aligned}$$ where $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.
  - Explanation: Bounds the height average directly, separating its mean-square contribution from a rapidly decaying pole contribution.
- **claim-structured-exceptional-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-dual)): There are constants $\delta,\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href="#eq:prime-convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prime-convolution">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Let $v,e\in\mathcal O\setminus\{0\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\le Y^\delta$, and let $1+\|u\|\le Y^{0.36}$. Put $$A(p,q)=\sum_{(b,e)=1}\beta(b)N(b)^{iu}\chi_b(vpq^2).$$ If $p,q$ run through coprime primary squarefree elements of respective norm lengths $P,Q\ge1$, and either $$\left\|\frac{\log P}{\log Y}-1\right\|\le\delta, \qquad 0\le\frac{\log Q}{\log Y}\le\delta,$$ or $$\left\|\frac{\log P}{\log Y}-\frac13\right\|\le\delta, \qquad \left\|\frac{\log Q}{\log Y}-\frac13\right\|\le\delta,$$ then $$\sum_{p,q}\|A(p,q)\|^2\ll Y^{7/3-\eta}. \label{eq:exceptional-moment}$$ The implied constant is uniform for the specified support and smoothness bounds.
  - Explanation: Obtains a uniform power saving near the exceptional conductor-length configurations for independently smooth prime convolutions, with small twists and exclusions.
- **claim-smooth-character-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality)): Let $\chi$ be a primitive nonprincipal Hecke character of trivial infinite parameter and conductor norm $D$. Let $W$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $\log Y$. The full sum $\sum_n W(N(n)/Z)\chi(n)N(n)^{it}$ admits a decomposition into dual norm dyads $$J\ll Y^\epsilon D(1+\|t\|)^2/Z$$ with amplitude $O(Y^\epsilon\sqrt{Z/J})$. The coefficients in a dual dyad are divisor-bounded, and its character is $\overline\chi$. All additional norm shifts can be integrated against a common conductor-independent $L^1$ majorant of mass $O(Y^\epsilon)$. The omitted tail is smaller than any prescribed negative power of $Y$, provided the lengths and heights are bounded by fixed powers of $Y$.
  - Explanation: Shortens a full smooth primitive nonprincipal character sum through its functional equation, with dual dyads and a common conductor-independent majorant.
- **claim-off-diagonal-character-gram** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-character-gram)): Let $p$ be primary squarefree with $N(p)\asymp P$, and let $W$ be a fixed smooth radial norm weight of compact support in $(0,\infty)$. For primary squarefree $p'$ of the same norm length put $$T_{p,p'}=\sum_{n\equiv1\, (3)} W(N(n)/Z)\chi_p(n)\overline{\chi_{p'}(n)}.$$ For $Z\ge1$ and every $\epsilon>0$, $$\sum_{p'\ne p}\|T_{p,p'}\|^2 \ll_\epsilon (PZ)^\epsilon Z\left(P+(P^3/Z)^{2/3}\right). \label{eq:near-orthogonality}$$ The same bound holds with a subset of the available $p'$.
  - Explanation: Controls the squared off-diagonal Gram row for radial smooth norm weights and squarefree character rows, including subsets of the available rows.
- **claim-corrected-and-averaged-dispersion** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-dispersion)): Under the preceding hypotheses the following assertions hold.* 1. *If $H\gg1$ and $\|u\|\le X^{0.17}$, and the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$, then $$\sum_a W_A(a)\|F_u(a)\|^2\ll \mathcal Q L^{O(1)}.$$* 2. *Suppose $\|u\|\le L^{O(1)}$, $H\gg1$, and $\beta$ has property *(SW)* and is supported on elements all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $C_r,C_g$ are sufficiently large, then for $H\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\sum_a\mu^2(a)W_A(a) \left\|F_u(a)-c_*\overline{G(a)}(Na)^{-1/6}S(u)\right\|^2 \ll \mathcal Q L^{-K}. \label{eq:dispersion-corrected}$$ For $1\ll H<L^{C_g}$ one has the uncorrected estimate $$\sum_a W_A(a)\|F_u(a)\|^2 \ll A\sum_b\|\beta_b\|^2+ \mathcal Q\left\{\left(B^{-1}\sum_b\|\beta_b\|\right)^2+L^{-K}\right\}. \label{eq:dispersion-short}$$ Here $1\ll H$ means only that $H$ is bounded below by a positive constant. In these low-height assertions the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$.* 3. *Suppose $u=\pm t+u_0$, $T\le \|t\|\le2T$, $2T+\|u_0\|\le X^{0.17}$, and $\|\partial^jW\|\ll_j L^{Cj}$ for fixed $C$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $T\ge L^{C_t}$ with $C_t$ sufficiently large, then $$\int_{T\le\|t\|\le2T}\sum_a W_A(a)\|F_{\pm t+u_0}(a)\|^2\frac{dt}{T} \le \int_{T\le\|t\|\le2T}\mathcal D_W\frac{dt}{T} +O(\mathcal Q L^{-K}), \label{eq:dispersion-height}$$ where the exact diagonal is $$\mathcal D_W=\sum_b\|\beta_b\|^2 \sum_{(a,b)=1}W_A(a).$$ No lower bound on $H$ is required here. When $B\le X^{0.40}$ the restriction on $u_0$ can be omitted.* *There is also an absolute sufficiently small $\gamma>0$ such that, when $B\le X^{0.40}$, $T\gg X^{0.01}$, and $\|\partial^jW\|\ll_j X^{j\gamma}$, the corresponding estimate is $$\int_{T\le\|t\|\le2T}\sum_a W_A(a)\|F_{\pm t+u_0}(a)\|^2\frac{dt}{T} \le \int_{T\le\|t\|\le2T}\mathcal D_W\frac{dt}{T} +O(\mathcal Q X^{-10\gamma}). \label{eq:dispersion-power}$$ This $\gamma$ is independent of the fixed powers in the divisor bounds. In the last two estimates, a radial support of width $O(J^{-1})$ gives $$\mathcal D_W\ll \frac AJ\sum_b\|\beta_b\|^2$$ for $J\le L^{O(1)}$, or $J\le X^\gamma$, respectively. In particular, when $B\le X^{0.40}$, the coefficients may be restricted sharply to any norm cell without changing these assertions.
  - Explanation: Combines coarse dispersion, corrected low-height variance, the short uncorrected estimate and height averages retaining the exact diagonal. Its coefficient, roughness, sequence and derivative restrictions depend on the asserted regime.
- **claim-exact-prime-decomposition** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-decomposition)): For either kind of height block, the prime sum with envelope $V$ has an exact decomposition into a number of Type I and bilinear terms bounded by a fixed power of $L$. This decomposition is valid with either kernel $$G(n)N(n)^{it} \quad\hbox{or}\quad c_*\mu^2(n)N(n)^{-1/6+it},$$ and uses the same coefficients for the two kernels. The coefficients are independent of $t$, divisor-bounded, and have all fixed absolute moments of the usual size $Y L^{O(1)}$ on a norm dyad of length $Y$. The resulting terms have the following properties.* 1. *Type I terms have an unrestricted inner variable $u$ and an outer variable $r$ with $$N(r)\asymp R,\qquad R\le X^{.40}\quad\hbox{on ordinary blocks},\qquad R\le X^{1/3-\kappa/2}\quad\hbox{on upper blocks}.$$ Their inner weight is a smooth envelope at length $X/R$, with fixed derivative bounds, depending permissibly on $r$.* 2. *Bilinear terms have independent coefficients $\alpha_a,\beta_b$, may be restricted individually to squarefree $a,b$, and satisfy $$N(a)\asymp A,\quad N(b)\asymp B,\quad AB\asymp X,\quad B\ll\sqrt X.$$ On ordinary blocks, $B\ge cX^{1/3}$ for some fixed $c>0$; on upper blocks, $B\ge X^{1/3-3\kappa}$. Whenever $B>X^{.40}$, the $b$ coefficient is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a bounded number of prime sequences on independent smooth norm dyads, each prime having norm at least a fixed positive power of $X$. There is no additional cutoff coupling those prime dyads.* 3. *On ordinary blocks, apart from the sparse terms in part (5), the $b$ coefficient is supported on numbers all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$ and satisfies the property <span class="upright">(SW)</span> required in Proposition <a href="#prop:dispersion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dispersion">13</a>. Here $C_r$ is any fixed constant chosen sufficiently large later.* 4. *For any fixed $C_g$, the ordinary bilinear terms with $$1\ll H:=B^2/A<L^{C_g}$$ consist of precisely three primes: one prime on the $b$ side and two specified prime dyads on the $a$ side. All three scales are $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, their weights and multiplicities are bounded, and the number of these terms is $O((1+\log L)^{O(1)})$. In each such term, $$\label{eq:decomposition-triple-moments} \sum_a\|\alpha_a\|^2\ll A/L^2, \qquad \sum_b\|\beta_b\|^2+\sum_b\|\beta_b\|\ll B/L.$$ All other nonsparse ordinary bilinear terms have $H\ge L^{C_g}$ for sufficiently large $X$.* 5. *The remaining ordinary terms have $X^{.35}\ll B<X^{.40}$ and a sparse $b$ support. Their total contribution, for either kernel and uniformly in $t$, is $O(X^{5/6-\eta})$ for some $\eta>0$ depending on the fixed parameters.* *The exponents in the number of terms and in the divisor bounds can be chosen independently of $C_r$ and $C_g$. All asserted identities hold before estimating either kernel.
  - Explanation: Decomposes the prime sum exactly for either squarefree-supported kernel, with independent height-independent coefficients, prescribed scale classes, a sparse remainder and an isolated prime transition.
- **claim-exact-prime-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-detector)): For squarefree $n$ on the envelope support and sufficiently large $X$, $$\label{eq:prime-detector} \boldsymbol 1_{\{n\ {\mathrm{prime}}\}} =\mathcal F_z(n)- \boldsymbol 1_{\{\Omega(n)=2\}}\mathcal F_z(n).$$ Moreover, $\mathcal F_z(n)$ is an exact sum of bounded-length distinguished prime tuples $$\label{eq:distinguished-tuples} \mathcal F_z(n)= \sum_{i\ge0}\frac1{i!} \sum_{n=\pi_1\cdots\pi_i h} \mathcal F_w(h)\prod_{j=1}^i (\psi_w(\pi_j)-\psi_z(\pi_j)).$$ Only boundedly many $i$ contribute, and the distinguished primes have norms in $[w,2z]$.
  - Explanation: Detects primes on squarefree envelope arguments by subtracting the semiprime contribution and expands the rough weight through bounded distinguished prime tuples.
- **claim-prime-scale-grouping** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-grouping)): The prime factors just obtained can be grouped into scales $A,B$, with $AB\asymp X$ and $B\ll\sqrt X$, as follows. On ordinary blocks either $B\ge X^{1/3+\delta_1}$ for some fixed $\delta_1>0$, or there are exactly three prime factors with exponents close to $1/3$, and $B$ is the largest of their three scales. On upper blocks one can always arrange $B\ge X^{1/3-3\kappa}$.
  - Explanation: Groups the exposed prime factors into admissible bilinear scales, isolating the exceptional balanced prime configuration on ordinary blocks.
- **claim-stopping-bin-separation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-stopping-separation)): The stopped terms are exact sums with independent coefficients on $a$ and $b$. The number of additional outer indices is a fixed power of $L$, and the coefficients have fixed divisor bounds independent of $C_r$.
  - Explanation: Shows that fixing the stopping bin and its endpoint counts gives exact independent coefficients, with bounded index complexity and divisor bounds independent of the roughness parameter.
- **claim-ordinary-sequence-condition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-decomposition-sw)): Every nonsparse ordinary $b$ coefficient satisfies <span class="upright">(SW)</span>: against any nonprincipal cubic Hecke character of the indicated trivial infinite parameter and primitive conductor of fixed polylogarithmic norm, its sum is $O_D(BL^{-D})$ for every fixed $D$. This remains true with the permitted polynomial-norm coprimality exclusions and fixed-polylogarithmic norm twists.
  - Explanation: Verifies the required character cancellation for every nonsparse ordinary coefficient, including the permitted coprimality exclusions and norm twists.
- **claim-low-height-bilinear-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bilinear-low)): Suppose $X^{.32}\le B\ll\sqrt X$, $H\gg1$, and the coefficient $\beta_b$ satisfies the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:dispersion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dispersion">13</a>. At low heights assume, in addition, its roughness and Siegel–Walfisz hypotheses. For every fixed $D,U>0$, the logarithmic parameters in that proposition can be chosen so that, uniformly for $\|t\|\le L^U$ and $H\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\sum_{a,b}\alpha_a\beta_b\mathcal D(ab)N(ab)^{it} V\!\left(\frac{N(ab)}X\right) \ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}. \label{eq:bilinear-low}$$ There is also the following transition estimate. Suppose $b$ is one prime, $a$ is a product of two primes, each of the three primes has its own specified smooth dyad of length $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, and their weights are bounded. If $1\ll H<L^{C_g}$, then the left side of <a href="#eq:bilinear-low" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bilinear-low">[eq:bilinear-low]</a> is $$O\!\left(X^{5/6}L^{-3/2}\right). \label{eq:bilinear-transition}$$ The constants are uniform for the families of dyads in Proposition <a href="#prop:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:decomposition">15</a>.
  - Explanation: Turns corrected dispersion into a model comparison with arbitrary logarithmic saving and separately controls the prime transition using its density savings.
- **claim-integrated-bilinear-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bilinear-height)): Let $X_0\asymp X$, and let $h_T$ be smooth, supported where $T\le \|t\|\le2T$, with $h_T^{(j)}(t)\ll_jT^{-j}$. Suppose $$L^{C_t}\le T\ll X^{1/6+\rho},\qquad B\ll\sqrt X,$$ and suppose that either $$\begin{aligned} T\ll X^{.01},&\qquad cX^{1/3}\le B,\quad c>0\text{ fixed},\tag{a}\\ T\gg X^{.01},&\qquad X^{1/3-3\kappa}\le B.\tag{b} \end{aligned}$$ Assume the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:dispersion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dispersion">13</a>, but impose neither roughness nor Siegel–Walfisz conditions. For every fixed $D>0$, by taking $C_t$ sufficiently large, $$\sum_{a,b}\alpha_a\beta_bG(ab)V\!\left(\frac{N(ab)}X\right) \int h_T(t)\left(\frac{N(ab)}{X_0}\right)^{it}\frac{dt}{T} \ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}. \label{eq:bilinear-integrated}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds integrated Gauss-sum bilinear terms across the stated height ranges without roughness or sequence assumptions, using localization in both norm variables.
- **claim-fourier-interval-truncation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-perron-truncation)): Let $1\le T\le X$, and let $V$ be a fixed smooth function supported in $(1/2,4)$, equal to one on a neighborhood of $[1,2]$. There is a smooth function $I_T$ such that $$I_T(s)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R} \eta(t/T)\frac{1-2^{-it}}{it}e^{its}\,dt, \label{eq:interval-fourier}$$ where $\eta$ is a fixed smooth cutoff supported in $[-1,1]$ and equal to one near zero. For every $m>0$, $$\left\|1_{[1,2]}(v)-V(v)I_T(\log v)\right\| \ll_m \sum_{b\in\{0,\log2\}} (1+T\|\log v-b\|)^{-m} \label{eq:interval-boundary-error}$$ on the support of $V$, and the difference is zero outside it. If coefficients collected at each integer norm satisfy $\|c_n\|\ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon$ for $X/2<n<4X$, then $$\sum_n \|c_n\|\left\|1_{[X,2X]}(n) -V(n/X)I_T(\log(n/X))\right\| \ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon(1+X/T). \label{eq:perron-error}$$
  - Explanation: Constructs a smooth Fourier truncation of the interval indicator and bounds the aggregate endpoint error when equal-norm coefficients obey the stated divisor-type bound.
- **claim-fixed-angular-prime-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-angular)): For every fixed integer $\ell$, $$\sum_{\substack{N\pi\le X\\\pi\equiv1\pmod3\ \mathrm{prime}}} \theta_\ell(\pi)\bigl(G(\pi)-c_*N(\pi)^{-1/6}\bigr) =o_\ell\!\left(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\right). \label{eq:angular-comparison}$$ In particular, for every fixed nonzero integer $\ell$, $$\sum_{\substack{N\pi\le X\\\pi\equiv1\pmod3\ \mathrm{prime}}} \theta_\ell(\pi)G(\pi) =o_\ell\!\left(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\right). \label{eq:angular-cancellation}$$
  - Explanation: Extends the prime-model comparison to each fixed integer angular mode and gives first-moment-scale cancellation for each fixed nonzero mode.
- **claim-fixed-infinity-hecke-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-hecke)): Put $k=\|\ell\|/2$. The complete primitive $L$-function has completion $$\label{eq:angular-hecke-fe} \Lambda(z,\Psi)=\left(\frac{\sqrt{3D}}{2\pi}\right)^z \Gamma(z+k)L(z,\Psi),\qquad \Lambda(z,\Psi)=\epsilon_\Psi\Lambda(1-z,\overline\Psi), \quad \|\epsilon_\Psi\|=1.$$ If $\ell\ne0$, or if $\ell=0$ and $\chi$ is nonprincipal cubic, the completed function is entire of order one. In these cases, for every fixed $A,M>0$, $$\label{eq:angular-prime-sw} \sum_{N\mathfrak p\le x}\Psi(\mathfrak p)\log N\mathfrak p \ll_{\ell,A,M}x(\log x)^{-M}, \qquad N\mathfrak f_\chi\le(\log x)^A.$$ Ramified primes have character value zero. Removing additional Euler factors is permitted with their explicit finite-prime error.
  - Explanation: States the primitive completion for fixed infinity type, its nonprincipal entireness cases and uniform logarithmic-conductor prime estimates with explicit treatment of omitted Euler factors.
- **claim-completed-angular-height-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-completed-height)): Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, put $R=Nr$, and let $W$ have fixed compact support in $(0,\infty)$ and uniformly bounded derivatives. For fixed $\ell$, define $$\mathcal C_{r,\ell,t}(V;W)= \sum_{\substack{d,u\\(d,r)=1}}\|d\|G(ru) \theta_\ell(rud^3)N(ud^3)^{it} W\left(\frac{N(ud^3)}V\right).$$ Suppose $T\ge2$, $V\gg1$, and $R,V,T$ are bounded by fixed powers of $X$. Write $$M_{r,\ell,t}(V;W)= \boldsymbol1_{\ell=0}A_0\mathcal MW(5/6+it)V^{5/6+it} \frac{\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}.$$ Then $$\int_{\|t\|\asymp T} \|\mathcal C_{r,\ell,t}(V;W)-M_{r,\ell,t}(V;W)\| \frac{dt}T \ll_{\ell,\epsilon}X^\epsilon\sqrt V\,R^{1/4}\sqrt T. \label{eq:angular-completed-height}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the mean absolute completed angular sum after subtracting its possible radial residue, under polynomial scale bounds and fixed smoothness.
- **claim-angular-type-i-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-typei)): Suppose $RU\asymp X$, $1\le R\ll X^{.51}$, and $(a_r)$ is divisor-bounded on $Nr\asymp R$. If $W_r$ has common fixed compact support in $(0,\infty)$ and each fixed derivative is bounded by a fixed logarithmic power uniformly in $r$, then $$\sum_r a_r\sum_u\mathcal D_\ell(ru)W_r(Nu/U) \ll_\ell X^{5/6-1/100}. \label{eq:angular-typeI-low}$$ For the high-height assertion suppose instead that the derivative bounds are fixed independently of $X$. For $2\le T\ll X^{1/6+\rho}$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\begin{aligned} \int_{\|t\|\asymp T}\sum_r\|a_r\| \left\|\sum_u G_\ell(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\right\|\frac{dt}T \ll_{\ell,\epsilon}{}& X^{1/2+\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\notag\\ &+\boldsymbol1_{\ell=0} O_D(X^{5/6}L^C T^{-D}), \label{eq:angular-typeI-high} \end{aligned}$$ where $\rho$ is the fixed parameter of <a href="#eq:power-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:power-parameters">[eq:power-parameters]</a> and $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.
  - Explanation: Proves the angular Type I model comparison and its height-average counterpart, retaining the radial pole term only in the zero angular mode.
- **claim-angular-exceptional-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-dual)): Proposition <a href="#prop:dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dual">10</a> holds with $\beta$ replaced by $\beta_\ell$. The constants $\delta,\eta$ and the implied constant may additionally depend on the fixed integer $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Transfers the structured exceptional moment estimate to angular prime convolutions while permitting dependence on the fixed mode.
- **claim-angular-sequence-condition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-sw)): Every nonsparse ordinary coefficient $\beta_0$ constructed in Proposition <a href="#prop:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:decomposition">15</a> satisfies <span class="upright">(SW)</span> after replacement by $\beta_\ell=\Theta_\ell\beta_0$.
  - Explanation: Checks the sequence condition for the actual angularly twisted ordinary decomposition coefficients; this is a separate verification rather than an abstract stability assertion.
- **claim-angular-dispersion-and-bilinear-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-dispersion)): The estimates of Proposition <a href="#prop:dispersion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dispersion">13</a> hold for $\beta_\ell$, assuming <span class="upright">(SW)</span> for $\beta_\ell$ wherever it was required. For $B>X^{.40}$, the stipulated structured class is exactly the angular prime-convolution class above. The conclusions of Propositions <a href="#prop:bilinear-low" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:bilinear-low">20</a> and <a href="#prop:bilinear-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:bilinear-height">21</a> consequently hold with the kernel multiplied by $\theta_\ell(ab)$ and with all their other hypotheses retained. The absolute small-$B$ parameter $\gamma$ is unchanged; other constants may depend on fixed $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Transfers dispersion and bilinear estimates to angular coefficients with their original structural restrictions and the twisted sequence condition, preserving the absolute small-length parameter.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve",
        "exact_quote": "For distinct primitive ray characters of trivial infinite parameter, with conductor norm at most $Q$ and with conductor above $3$ dividing a fixed modulus, and coefficients supported on a norm dyad of length $Z$, one has $$\\sum_\\chi\\left|\\sum_n v_n\\chi(n)\\right|^2\n \\ll (Z+Q^2)\\sum_n|v_n|^2.$$ The implied constant is uniform when a fixed additional modulus above $3$ is allowed. In particular, the bound applies to the cubic characters indexed by coprime primary squarefree $p,q$ through $b\\mapsto\\chi_b(pq^2)$, whose conductor away from $3$ is $pq$. There is no $Q^\\epsilon$ loss.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve",
          "exact_quote": "For distinct primitive ray characters of trivial infinite parameter, with conductor norm at most $Q$ and with conductor above $3$ dividing a fixed modulus, and coefficients supported on a norm dyad of length $Z$, one has $$\\sum_\\chi\\left|\\sum_n v_n\\chi(n)\\right|^2\n \\ll (Z+Q^2)\\sum_n|v_n|^2.$$ The implied constant is uniform when a fixed additional modulus above $3$ is allowed. In particular, the bound applies to the cubic characters indexed by coprime primary squarefree $p,q$ through $b\\mapsto\\chi_b(pq^2)$, whose conductor away from $3$ is $pq$. There is no $Q^\\epsilon$ loss.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cubic-extensions",
        "exact_quote": "The following estimates allow an arbitrarily small $X^\\epsilon$ loss. First, for divisor-bounded coefficients $(v_n)$ of norm length $Z$, without a squarefree restriction, $$\\sum_{Na\\asymp P}\\mu^2(a)\n       \\left|\\sum_n v_n\\chi_a(n)\\right|^2\n \\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon Z\n       \\bigl(P+Z+(PZ)^{2/3}\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:inner-extension}$$ Second, for coefficients $(u_b)$ on primary squarefree elements of norm length $P$, $$\\sum_{Na\\asymp J}\\left|\\sum_b u_b\\chi_b(a)\\right|^2\n \\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon\n       \\bigl(J+(JP)^{2/3}+J^{1/3}P\\bigr)\\sum_b|u_b|^2.\n \\label{eq:outer-extension}$$ The latter also holds when $a$ ranges over all nonzero elements of $\\mathcal O$ of norm $\\asymp J$. If $J\\gg P$, its last term is absorbed by $(JP)^{2/3}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cubic-extensions",
          "exact_quote": "The following estimates allow an arbitrarily small $X^\\epsilon$ loss.           "source_id": "PM-023.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-voronoi-background",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, and let $W$ be smooth and compactly supported in $(0,\\infty)$. Put $R=Nr$ and $A_0=(2\\pi)^{5/3}/(3^{7/2}\\Gamma(2/3))$. The completed sum $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{d,u\\\\(d,r)=1}}|d|G(ur)\n             W\\left(\\frac{N(ud^3)}{V}\\right)$$ has main term $A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\varphi(r)R^{-7/6}$. Its remaining term is $$\\frac{R^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\\pi)^2}\n \\sum_{\\substack{0\\ne\\nu\\in\\lambda^{-1}\\mathcal O, d\\\\(d,r)=1}}\n \\frac{a_r(\\nu)b_r(\\nu)}{N\\nu\\,(Nd)^{5/2}}\n \\check W\\left(\\frac{(2\\pi)^4N(d^3\\nu)V}{R^2}\\right),$$ where, for sufficiently small $\\sigma>0$, $$\\check W(v)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\\int_{(-\\sigma)}v^z\n \\frac{\\Gamma(5/6-z)\\Gamma(7/6-z)}\n      {\\Gamma(z-1/6)\\Gamma(z+1/6)}\\mathcal MW(z)\\,dz.$$ The coefficients depend only on $r$ and $\\nu$, independently of $V$ and $W$. They vanish outside representations $$\\nu=\\lambda^j\\zeta h w(h')^3,\\quad j\\ge-1,\\quad\n h,h'\\mid r^\\infty,\\quad (w,r)=1,\\quad \\mu^2(hw)=1,$$ where $h,h',w$ are primary and $\\zeta$ is a unit. On this support, they are $$a_r(\\nu)=a^\\star(\\lambda^{-3}\\nu),\\qquad\n b_r(\\nu)=\\mu\\left(\\frac{r}{(\\lambda\\nu,r)}\\right)\n          \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{\\varphi\\left(r/(\\lambda\\nu,r)\\right)},$$ where $a^\\star$ is the theta coefficient in [[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (5.74)]; the second formula is its Equation (5.68). The index $\\lambda^{-3}\\nu$ is the one supplied by its coefficient identity (5.79). They satisfy $$|a_r(\\nu)|\\ll 3^{\\max(j,0)/3}|h'|,\n \\qquad |b_r(\\nu)|\\le N((\\lambda\\nu,r)).$$ In particular, if $V,V^{-1},R\\ll X^{C_0}$ for fixed $C_0$, $W$ is supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, and each fixed derivative bound is at most a fixed logarithmic power, then $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}\n +O_\\epsilon(X^\\epsilon R^{1/2}).\n \\label{eq:voronoi}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-voronoi-background",

They vanish outside representations $$\\nu=\\lambda^j\\zeta h w(h')^3,\\quad j\\ge-1,\\quad\n h,h'\\mid r^\\infty,\\quad (w,r)=1,\\quad \\mu^2(hw)=1,$$ where $h,h',w$ are primary and $\\zeta$ is a unit. On this support, they are $$a_r(\\nu)=a^\\star(\\lambda^{-3}\\nu),\\qquad\n b_r(\\nu)=\\mu\\left(\\frac{r}{(\\lambda\\nu,r)}\\right)\n          \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{\\varphi\\left(r/(\\lambda\\nu,r)\\right)},$$ where $a^\\star$ is the theta coefficient in [[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (5.74)]; the second formula is its Equation (5.68). The index $\\lambda^{-3}\\nu$ is the one supplied by its coefficient identity (5.79). They satisfy $$|a_r(\\nu)|\\ll 3^{\\max(j,0)/3}|h'|,\n \\qquad |b_r(\\nu)|\\le N((\\lambda\\nu,r)).$$ In particular, if $V,V^{-1},R\\ll X^{C_0}$ for fixed $C_0$, $W$ is supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, and each fixed derivative bound is at most a fixed logarithmic power, then $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}\n +O_\\epsilon(X^\\epsilon R^{1/2}).\n \\label{eq:voronoi}$$",

      "id": "claim-level-voronoi",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-voronoi-background"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-hb-height",
        "exact_quote": "For primary squarefree $r$, the Dirichlet series $$f_r(s)=\\sum_u G(ru)(Nu)^{-s},\\qquad \\operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ continues meromorphically to $\\operatorname{Re}s>1/2$, with at most a simple pole at $5/6$. It has polynomial growth in closed strips there, away from its pole. For sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ and every $T\\ge1$, $$\\int_{-T}^T|f_r(1/2+\\epsilon+it)|^2dt\n \\ll_\\epsilon (Nr)^{1/2+4\\epsilon}T^2.\n \\label{eq:hb-mean-square}$$ Its residue $\\rho_r$ satisfies $|\\rho_r|\\ll (Nr)^{-1/6}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-hb-height",
          "exact_quote": "For primary squarefree $r$, the Dirichlet series $$f_r(s)=\\sum_u G(ru)(Nu)^{-s},\\qquad \\operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ continues meromorphically to $\\operatorname{Re}s>1/2$, with at most a simple pole at $5/6$. It has polynomial growth in closed strips there, away from its pole. For sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ and every $T\\ge1$, $$\\int_{-T}^T|f_r(1/2+\\epsilon+it)|^2dt\n \\ll_\\epsilon (Nr)^{1/2+4\\epsilon}T^2.\n \\label{eq:hb-mean-square}$$ Its residue $\\rho_r$ satisfies $|\\rho_r|\\ll (Nr)^{-1/6}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For primary squarefree $r$, the Dirichlet series $$f_r(s)=\\sum_u G(ru)(Nu)^{-s},\\qquad \\operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ continues meromorphically to $\\operatorname{Re}s>1/2$, with at most a simple pole at $5/6$. It has polynomial growth in closed strips there, away from its pole.       "explanation": "Supplies meromorphic continuation, strip growth, a height mean-square bound and residue control for squarefree levels.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-typei",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$ and $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$. Let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded and supported on $Nr\\asymp R$. Let $W_r$ have support in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $L$, uniformly in $r$. Then $$\\sum_r a_r\\sum_u\n \\left(G(ru)-c_*\\frac{\\mu^2(ru)}{N(ru)^{1/6}}\\right)W_r(Nu/U)\n \\ll X^{5/6-\\eta}$$ for some fixed $\\eta>0$. One may take $\\eta=1/100$, with constants depending on the stated fixed parameters.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-typei",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$ and $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$. Let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded and supported on $Nr\\asymp R$. Then $$\\sum_r a_r\\sum_u\n \\left(G(ru)-c_*\\frac{\\mu^2(ru)}{N(ru)^{1/6}}\\right)W_r(Nu/U)\n \\ll X^{5/6-\\eta}$$ for some fixed $\\eta>0$. One may take $\\eta=1/100$, with constants depending on the stated fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$ and $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$. Let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded and supported on $Nr\\asymp R$. One may take $\\eta=1/100$, with constants depending on the stated fixed parameters.",
      "explanation": "Compares the Gauss-sum kernel with its squarefree model for divisor-bounded outer coefficients and logarithmically smooth inner weights, with the stated fixed power saving.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-typei",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-typeiheight",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, and let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$. Suppose the smooth compactly supported weights $W_r$ have fixed support ratios and uniformly bounded derivatives, independently of $X$. For $T\\ge2$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\\sum_{Nr\\asymp R}|a_r|\n       \\left|\\sum_uG(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\\right|\\frac{dt}{T}\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\ll_\\epsilon\n       X^{1/2+\\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\n       +O_D\\bigl(X^{5/6}L^{C}T^{-D}\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:typeI-height}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-typeiheight",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, and let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$.           "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, and let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\delta,\\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href=\"#eq:prime-convolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prime-convolution\">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Let $v,e\\in\\mathcal O\\setminus\\{0\\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\\le Y^\\delta$, and let $1+|u|\\le Y^{0.36}$. Put $$A(p,q)=\\sum_{(b,e)=1}\\beta(b)N(b)^{iu}\\chi_b(vpq^2).$$ If $p,q$ run through coprime primary squarefree elements of respective norm lengths $P,Q\\ge1$, and either $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-1\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}\\le\\delta,$$ or $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,$$ then $$\\sum_{p,q}|A(p,q)|^2\\ll Y^{7/3-\\eta}.\n \\label{eq:exceptional-moment}$$ The implied constant is uniform for the specified support and smoothness bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\delta,\\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href=\"#eq:prime-convolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prime-convolution\">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Let $v,e\\in\\mathcal O\\setminus\\{0\\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\\le Y^\\delta$, and let $1+|u|\\le Y^{0.36}$.       "exact_statement": "There are constants $\\delta,\\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href=\"#eq:prime-convolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prime-convolution\">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Put $$A(p,q)=\\sum_{(b,e)=1}\\beta(b)N(b)^{iu}\\chi_b(vpq^2).$$ If $p,q$ run through coprime primary squarefree elements of respective norm lengths $P,Q\\ge1$, and either $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-1\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}\\le\\delta,$$ or $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,$$ then $$\\sum_{p,q}|A(p,q)|^2\\ll Y^{7/3-\\eta}.\n \\label{eq:exceptional-moment}$$ The implied constant is uniform for the specified support and smoothness bounds.",
      "explanation": "Obtains a uniform power saving near the exceptional conductor-length configurations for independently smooth prime convolutions, with small twists and exclusions.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",
          "exact_quote": "Let $v,e\\in\\mathcal O\\setminus\\{0\\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\\le Y^\\delta$, and let $1+|u|\\le Y^{0.36}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "eq:prime-convolution",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive constant $c$, a positive integer $k_0$, and fixed support and smoothness bounds. An admissible prime convolution at length $Y$ is $$\\beta(b)=\\mu^2(b)\n \\sum_{b=\\pi_1\\cdots\\pi_k}\n       \\prod_{j=1}^k w_j\\bigl(N(\\pi_j)/F_j\\bigr),\n \\qquad 1\\le k\\le k_0,\\qquad \\prod_{j=1}^k F_j\\asymp Y,\n \\label{eq:prime-convolution}$$ where the primes are primary, the tuple is ordered, every supported prime has norm at least $Y^c$, and the $w_j$ have compact support in fixed subintervals of $(0,\\infty)$. Each fixed derivative is bounded by a fixed power of $\\log Y$, uniformly over the family under consideration. These bounds imply fixed divisor bounds for all coefficients below.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-structured-exceptional-moments",

      "quantifiers": [

    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\chi$ be a primitive nonprincipal Hecke character of trivial infinite parameter and conductor norm $D$. Let $W$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $\\log Y$. The full sum $\\sum_n W(N(n)/Z)\\chi(n)N(n)^{it}$ admits a decomposition into dual norm dyads $$J\\ll Y^\\epsilon D(1+|t|)^2/Z$$ with amplitude $O(Y^\\epsilon\\sqrt{Z/J})$. The coefficients in a dual dyad are divisor-bounded, and its character is $\\overline\\chi$. All additional norm shifts can be integrated against a common conductor-independent $L^1$ majorant of mass $O(Y^\\epsilon)$. The omitted tail is smaller than any prescribed negative power of $Y$, provided the lengths and heights are bounded by fixed powers of $Y$.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\chi$ be a primitive nonprincipal Hecke character of trivial infinite parameter and conductor norm $D$. The coefficients in a dual dyad are divisor-bounded, and its character is $\\overline\\chi$. All additional norm shifts can be integrated against a common conductor-independent $L^1$ majorant of mass $O(Y^\\epsilon)$. The omitted tail is smaller than any prescribed negative power of $Y$, provided the lengths and heights are bounded by fixed powers of $Y$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-character-gram",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be primary squarefree with $N(p)\\asymp P$, and let $W$ be a fixed smooth radial norm weight of compact support in $(0,\\infty)$. For primary squarefree $p'$ of the same norm length put $$T_{p,p'}=\\sum_{n\\equiv1\\, (3)}\n     W(N(n)/Z)\\chi_p(n)\\overline{\\chi_{p'}(n)}.$$ For $Z\\ge1$ and every $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\sum_{p'\\ne p}|T_{p,p'}|^2\n \\ll_\\epsilon (PZ)^\\epsilon\n      Z\\left(P+(P^3/Z)^{2/3}\\right).\n \\label{eq:near-orthogonality}$$ The same bound holds with a subset of the available $p'$.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-character-gram",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be primary squarefree with $N(p)\\asymp P$, and let $W$ be a fixed smooth radial norm weight of compact support in $(0,\\infty)$.           "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $p$ be primary squarefree with $N(p)\\asymp P$, and let $W$ be a fixed smooth radial norm weight of compact support in $(0,\\infty)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-dispersion",
        "exact_quote": "Under the preceding hypotheses the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $H\\gg1$ and $|u|\\le X^{0.17}$, and the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$, then $$\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_u(a)|^2\\ll \\mathcal Q L^{O(1)}.$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $|u|\\le L^{O(1)}$, $H\\gg1$, and $\\beta$ has property *(SW)* and is supported on elements all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $C_r,C_g$ are sufficiently large, then for $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_a\\mu^2(a)W_A(a)\n     \\left|F_u(a)-c_*\\overline{G(a)}(Na)^{-1/6}S(u)\\right|^2\n     \\ll \\mathcal Q L^{-K}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-corrected}$$ For $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$ one has the uncorrected estimate $$\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_u(a)|^2\n     \\ll A\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\n     \\mathcal Q\\left\\{\\left(B^{-1}\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\right)^2+L^{-K}\\right\\}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-short}$$ Here $1\\ll H$ means only that $H$ is bounded below by a positive constant. In these low-height assertions the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$.*\n\n\n3.  *Suppose $u=\\pm t+u_0$, $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, $2T+|u_0|\\le X^{0.17}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j L^{Cj}$ for fixed $C$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $T\\ge L^{C_t}$ with $C_t$ sufficiently large, then $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q L^{-K}),\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-height}$$ where the exact diagonal is $$\\mathcal D_W=\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2\n                      \\sum_{(a,b)=1}W_A(a).$$ No lower bound on $H$ is required here. When $B\\le X^{0.40}$ the restriction on $u_0$ can be omitted.*\n\n\n    *There is also an absolute sufficiently small $\\gamma>0$ such that, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, $T\\gg X^{0.01}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j X^{j\\gamma}$, the corresponding estimate is $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q X^{-10\\gamma}).\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-power}$$ This $\\gamma$ is independent of the fixed powers in the divisor bounds. In the last two estimates, a radial support of width $O(J^{-1})$ gives $$\\mathcal D_W\\ll \\frac AJ\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2$$ for $J\\le L^{O(1)}$, or $J\\le X^\\gamma$, respectively. In particular, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, the coefficients may be restricted sharply to any norm cell without changing these assertions.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For every fixed $K>0$, if $C_r,C_g$ are sufficiently large, then for $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_a\\mu^2(a)W_A(a)\n     \\left|F_u(a)-c_*\\overline{G(a)}(Na)^{-1/6}S(u)\\right|^2\n     \\ll \\mathcal Q L^{-K}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-corrected}$$ For $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$ one has the uncorrected estimate $$\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_u(a)|^2\n     \\ll A\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\n     \\mathcal Q\\left\\{\\left(B^{-1}\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\right)^2+L^{-K}\\right\\}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-short}$$ Here $1\\ll H$ means only that $H$ is bounded below by a positive constant. In these low-height assertions the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$.*\n\n\n3.  *Suppose $u=\\pm t+u_0$, $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, $2T+|u_0|\\le X^{0.17}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j L^{Cj}$ for fixed $C$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $T\\ge L^{C_t}$ with $C_t$ sufficiently large, then $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q L^{-K}),\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-height}$$ where the exact diagonal is $$\\mathcal D_W=\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2\n                      \\sum_{(a,b)=1}W_A(a).$$ No lower bound on $H$ is required here. When $B\\le X^{0.40}$ the restriction on $u_0$ can be omitted.*\n\n\n    *There is also an absolute sufficiently small $\\gamma>0$ such that, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, $T\\gg X^{0.01}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j X^{j\\gamma}$, the corresponding estimate is $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q X^{-10\\gamma}).\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-power}$$ This $\\gamma$ is independent of the fixed powers in the divisor bounds. In the last two estimates, a radial support of width $O(J^{-1})$ gives $$\\mathcal D_W\\ll \\frac AJ\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2$$ for $J\\le L^{O(1)}$, or $J\\le X^\\gamma$, respectively. When $B\\le X^{0.40}$ the restriction on $u_0$ can be omitted.*\n\n\n    *There is also an absolute sufficiently small $\\gamma>0$ such that, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, $T\\gg X^{0.01}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j X^{j\\gamma}$, the corresponding estimate is $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q X^{-10\\gamma}).\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-power}$$ This $\\gamma$ is independent of the fixed powers in the divisor bounds. In the last two estimates, a radial support of width $O(J^{-1})$ gives $$\\mathcal D_W\\ll \\frac AJ\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2$$ for $J\\le L^{O(1)}$, or $J\\le X^\\gamma$, respectively. In particular, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, the coefficients may be restricted sharply to any norm cell without changing these assertions.",
      "explanation": "Combines coarse dispersion, corrected low-height variance, the short uncorrected estimate and height averages retaining the exact diagonal. Its coefficient, roughness, sequence and derivative restrictions depend on the asserted regime.",

        {
          "anchor": "sec:dispersion",
          "exact_quote": "When $B>X^{0.40}$, suppose in addition that $\\beta$ is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a fixed number of prime sequences of the kind in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dual\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dual\">10</a>, each prime having norm at least $X^c$ for some fixed $c>0$.",

      "id": "claim-corrected-and-averaged-dispersion",
      "kind": "bound",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-decomposition",
        "exact_quote": "For either kind of height block, the prime sum with envelope $V$ has an exact decomposition into a number of Type I and bilinear terms bounded by a fixed power of $L$. This decomposition is valid with either kernel $$G(n)N(n)^{it}\n \\quad\\hbox{or}\\quad\n c_*\\mu^2(n)N(n)^{-1/6+it},$$ and uses the same coefficients for the two kernels. The coefficients are independent of $t$, divisor-bounded, and have all fixed absolute moments of the usual size $Y L^{O(1)}$ on a norm dyad of length $Y$. The resulting terms have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Type I terms have an unrestricted inner variable $u$ and an outer variable $r$ with $$N(r)\\asymp R,\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{.40}\\quad\\hbox{on ordinary blocks},\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{1/3-\\kappa/2}\\quad\\hbox{on upper blocks}.$$ Their inner weight is a smooth envelope at length $X/R$, with fixed derivative bounds, depending permissibly on $r$.*\n\n2.  *Bilinear terms have independent coefficients $\\alpha_a,\\beta_b$, may be restricted individually to squarefree $a,b$, and satisfy $$N(a)\\asymp A,\\quad N(b)\\asymp B,\\quad AB\\asymp X,\\quad B\\ll\\sqrt X.$$ On ordinary blocks, $B\\ge cX^{1/3}$ for some fixed $c>0$; on upper blocks, $B\\ge X^{1/3-3\\kappa}$. Whenever $B>X^{.40}$, the $b$ coefficient is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a bounded number of prime sequences on independent smooth norm dyads, each prime having norm at least a fixed positive power of $X$. There is no additional cutoff coupling those prime dyads.*\n\n3.  *On ordinary blocks, apart from the sparse terms in part (5), the $b$ coefficient is supported on numbers all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$ and satisfies the property <span class=\"upright\">(SW)</span> required in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. Here $C_r$ is any fixed constant chosen sufficiently large later.*\n\n\n4.  *For any fixed $C_g$, the ordinary bilinear terms with $$1\\ll H:=B^2/A<L^{C_g}$$ consist of precisely three primes: one prime on the $b$ side and two specified prime dyads on the $a$ side. All three scales are $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, their weights and multiplicities are bounded, and the number of these terms is $O((1+\\log L)^{O(1)})$. In each such term, $$\\label{eq:decomposition-triple-moments}\n     \\sum_a|\\alpha_a|^2\\ll A/L^2,\n     \\qquad\n     \\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\ll B/L.$$ All other nonsparse ordinary bilinear terms have $H\\ge L^{C_g}$ for sufficiently large $X$.*\n\n5.  *The remaining ordinary terms have $X^{.35}\\ll B<X^{.40}$ and a sparse $b$ support. Their total contribution, for either kernel and uniformly in $t$, is $O(X^{5/6-\\eta})$ for some $\\eta>0$ depending on the fixed parameters.*\n\n*The exponents in the number of terms and in the divisor bounds can be chosen independently of $C_r$ and $C_g$. All asserted identities hold before estimating either kernel.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-decomposition",
          "exact_quote": "For either kind of height block, the prime sum with envelope $V$ has an exact decomposition into a number of Type I and bilinear terms bounded by a fixed power of $L$. This decomposition is valid with either kernel $$G(n)N(n)^{it}\n \\quad\\hbox{or}\\quad\n c_*\\mu^2(n)N(n)^{-1/6+it},$$ and uses the same coefficients for the two kernels. The coefficients are independent of $t$, divisor-bounded, and have all fixed absolute moments of the usual size $Y L^{O(1)}$ on a norm dyad of length $Y$. The resulting terms have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Type I terms have an unrestricted inner variable $u$ and an outer variable $r$ with $$N(r)\\asymp R,\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{.40}\\quad\\hbox{on ordinary blocks},\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{1/3-\\kappa/2}\\quad\\hbox{on upper blocks}.$$ Their inner weight is a smooth envelope at length $X/R$, with fixed derivative bounds, depending permissibly on $r$.*\n\n2.  *Bilinear terms have independent coefficients $\\alpha_a,\\beta_b$, may be restricted individually to squarefree $a,b$, and satisfy $$N(a)\\asymp A,\\quad N(b)\\asymp B,\\quad AB\\asymp X,\\quad B\\ll\\sqrt X.$$ On ordinary blocks, $B\\ge cX^{1/3}$ for some fixed $c>0$; on upper blocks, $B\\ge X^{1/3-3\\kappa}$. Whenever $B>X^{.40}$, the $b$ coefficient is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a bounded number of prime sequences on independent smooth norm dyads, each prime having norm at least a fixed positive power of $X$. There is no additional cutoff coupling those prime dyads.*\n\n3.  *On ordinary blocks, apart from the sparse terms in part (5), the $b$ coefficient is supported on numbers all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$ and satisfies the property <span class=\"upright\">(SW)</span> required in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. Here $C_r$ is any fixed constant chosen sufficiently large later.*\n\n\n4.  *For any fixed $C_g$, the ordinary bilinear terms with $$1\\ll H:=B^2/A<L^{C_g}$$ consist of precisely three primes: one prime on the $b$ side and two specified prime dyads on the $a$ side. All three scales are $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, their weights and multiplicities are bounded, and the number of these terms is $O((1+\\log L)^{O(1)})$. In each such term, $$\\label{eq:decomposition-triple-moments}\n     \\sum_a|\\alpha_a|^2\\ll A/L^2,\n     \\qquad\n     \\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\ll B/L.$$ All other nonsparse ordinary bilinear terms have $H\\ge L^{C_g}$ for sufficiently large $X$.*\n\n5.  *The remaining ordinary terms have $X^{.35}\\ll B<X^{.40}$ and a sparse $b$ support. Their total contribution, for either kernel and uniformly in $t$, is $O(X^{5/6-\\eta})$ for some $\\eta>0$ depending on the fixed parameters.*\n\n*The exponents in the number of terms and in the divisor bounds can be chosen independently of $C_r$ and $C_g$. All asserted identities hold before estimating either kernel.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-detector",
        "exact_quote": "For squarefree $n$ on the envelope support and sufficiently large $X$, $$\\label{eq:prime-detector}\n \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{n\\ {\\mathrm{prime}}\\}}\n =\\mathcal F_z(n)-\n  \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{\\Omega(n)=2\\}}\\mathcal F_z(n).$$ Moreover, $\\mathcal F_z(n)$ is an exact sum of bounded-length distinguished prime tuples $$\\label{eq:distinguished-tuples}\n \\mathcal F_z(n)=\n \\sum_{i\\ge0}\\frac1{i!}\n \\sum_{n=\\pi_1\\cdots\\pi_i h}\n \\mathcal F_w(h)\\prod_{j=1}^i\n       (\\psi_w(\\pi_j)-\\psi_z(\\pi_j)).$$ Only boundedly many $i$ contribute, and the distinguished primes have norms in $[w,2z]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-detector",
          "exact_quote": "For squarefree $n$ on the envelope support and sufficiently large $X$, $$\\label{eq:prime-detector}\n \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{n\\ {\\mathrm{prime}}\\}}\n =\\mathcal F_z(n)-\n  \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{\\Omega(n)=2\\}}\\mathcal F_z(n).$$ Moreover, $\\mathcal F_z(n)$ is an exact sum of bounded-length distinguished prime tuples $$\\label{eq:distinguished-tuples}\n \\mathcal F_z(n)=\n \\sum_{i\\ge0}\\frac1{i!}\n \\sum_{n=\\pi_1\\cdots\\pi_i h}\n \\mathcal F_w(h)\\prod_{j=1}^i\n       (\\psi_w(\\pi_j)-\\psi_z(\\pi_j)).$$ Only boundedly many $i$ contribute, and the distinguished primes have norms in $[w,2z]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For squarefree $n$ on the envelope support and sufficiently large $X$, $$\\label{eq:prime-detector}\n \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{n\\ {\\mathrm{prime}}\\}}\n =\\mathcal F_z(n)-\n  \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{\\Omega(n)=2\\}}\\mathcal F_z(n).$$ Moreover, $\\mathcal F_z(n)$ is an exact sum of bounded-length distinguished prime tuples $$\\label{eq:distinguished-tuples}\n \\mathcal F_z(n)=\n \\sum_{i\\ge0}\\frac1{i!}\n \\sum_{n=\\pi_1\\cdots\\pi_i h}\n \\mathcal F_w(h)\\prod_{j=1}^i\n       (\\psi_w(\\pi_j)-\\psi_z(\\pi_j)).$$ Only boundedly many $i$ contribute, and the distinguished primes have norms in $[w,2z]$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-grouping",
        "exact_quote": "The prime factors just obtained can be grouped into scales $A,B$, with $AB\\asymp X$ and $B\\ll\\sqrt X$, as follows. On ordinary blocks either $B\\ge X^{1/3+\\delta_1}$ for some fixed $\\delta_1>0$, or there are exactly three prime factors with exponents close to $1/3$, and $B$ is the largest of their three scales.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-low",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $X^{.32}\\le B\\ll\\sqrt X$, $H\\gg1$, and the coefficient $\\beta_b$ satisfies the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. At low heights assume, in addition, its roughness and Siegel–Walfisz hypotheses. For every fixed $D,U>0$, the logarithmic parameters in that proposition can be chosen so that, uniformly for $|t|\\le L^U$ and $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_b\\mathcal D(ab)N(ab)^{it}\n                 V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-low}$$ There is also the following transition estimate. Suppose $b$ is one prime, $a$ is a product of two primes, each of the three primes has its own specified smooth dyad of length $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, and their weights are bounded. If $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$, then the left side of <a href=\"#eq:bilinear-low\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bilinear-low\">[eq:bilinear-low]</a> is $$O\\!\\left(X^{5/6}L^{-3/2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:bilinear-transition}$$ The constants are uniform for the families of dyads in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:decomposition\">15</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-low",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $X^{.32}\\le B\\ll\\sqrt X$, $H\\gg1$, and the coefficient $\\beta_b$ satisfies the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. At low heights assume, in addition, its roughness and Siegel–Walfisz hypotheses. For every fixed $D,U>0$, the logarithmic parameters in that proposition can be chosen so that, uniformly for $|t|\\le L^U$ and $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_b\\mathcal D(ab)N(ab)^{it}\n                 V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-low}$$ There is also the following transition estimate. Suppose $b$ is one prime, $a$ is a product of two primes, each of the three primes has its own specified smooth dyad of length $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, and their weights are bounded. If $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$, then the left side of <a href=\"#eq:bilinear-low\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bilinear-low\">[eq:bilinear-low]</a> is $$O\\!\\left(X^{5/6}L^{-3/2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:bilinear-transition}$$ The constants are uniform for the families of dyads in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:decomposition\">15</a>.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-height",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X_0\\asymp X$, and let $h_T$ be smooth, supported where $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, with $h_T^{(j)}(t)\\ll_jT^{-j}$. Suppose $$L^{C_t}\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho},\\qquad B\\ll\\sqrt X,$$ and suppose that either $$\\begin{aligned}\n T\\ll X^{.01},&\\qquad cX^{1/3}\\le B,\\quad c>0\\text{ fixed},\\tag{a}\\\\\n T\\gg X^{.01},&\\qquad X^{1/3-3\\kappa}\\le B.\\tag{b}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Assume the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>, but impose neither roughness nor Siegel–Walfisz conditions. For every fixed $D>0$, by taking $C_t$ sufficiently large, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_bG(ab)V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n \\int h_T(t)\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}{X_0}\\right)^{it}\\frac{dt}{T}\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-integrated}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-height",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X_0\\asymp X$, and let $h_T$ be smooth, supported where $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, with $h_T^{(j)}(t)\\ll_jT^{-j}$. For every fixed $D>0$, by taking $C_t$ sufficiently large, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_bG(ab)V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n \\int h_T(t)\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}{X_0}\\right)^{it}\\frac{dt}{T}\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-integrated}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X_0\\asymp X$, and let $h_T$ be smooth, supported where $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, with $h_T^{(j)}(t)\\ll_jT^{-j}$. Suppose $$L^{C_t}\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho},\\qquad B\\ll\\sqrt X,$$ and suppose that either $$\\begin{aligned}\n T\\ll X^{.01},&\\qquad cX^{1/3}\\le B,\\quad c>0\\text{ fixed},\\tag{a}\\\\\n T\\gg X^{.01},&\\qquad X^{1/3-3\\kappa}\\le B.\\tag{b}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Assume the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>, but impose neither roughness nor Siegel–Walfisz conditions. For every fixed $D>0$, by taking $C_t$ sufficiently large, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_bG(ab)V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n \\int h_T(t)\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}{X_0}\\right)^{it}\\frac{dt}{T}\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-integrated}$$",
      "explanation": "Bounds integrated Gauss-sum bilinear terms across the stated height ranges without roughness or sequence assumptions, using localization in both norm variables.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le T\\le X$, and let $V$ be a fixed smooth function supported in $(1/2,4)$, equal to one on a neighborhood of $[1,2]$. There is a smooth function $I_T$ such that $$I_T(s)=\\frac1{2\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb R}\n       \\eta(t/T)\\frac{1-2^{-it}}{it}e^{its}\\,dt,\n \\label{eq:interval-fourier}$$ where $\\eta$ is a fixed smooth cutoff supported in $[-1,1]$ and equal to one near zero. For every $m>0$, $$\\left|1_{[1,2]}(v)-V(v)I_T(\\log v)\\right|\n \\ll_m \\sum_{b\\in\\{0,\\log2\\}}\n             (1+T|\\log v-b|)^{-m}\n \\label{eq:interval-boundary-error}$$ on the support of $V$, and the difference is zero outside it. If coefficients collected at each integer norm satisfy $|c_n|\\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon$ for $X/2<n<4X$, then $$\\sum_n |c_n|\\left|1_{[X,2X]}(n)\n            -V(n/X)I_T(\\log(n/X))\\right|\n       \\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon(1+X/T).\n \\label{eq:perron-error}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-angular",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed integer $\\ell$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{N\\pi\\le X\\\\\\pi\\equiv1\\pmod3\\ \\mathrm{prime}}}\n \\theta_\\ell(\\pi)\\bigl(G(\\pi)-c_*N(\\pi)^{-1/6}\\bigr)\n =o_\\ell\\!\\left(\\frac{X^{5/6}}{\\log X}\\right).\n \\label{eq:angular-comparison}$$ In particular, for every fixed nonzero integer $\\ell$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{N\\pi\\le X\\\\\\pi\\equiv1\\pmod3\\ \\mathrm{prime}}}\n       \\theta_\\ell(\\pi)G(\\pi)\n =o_\\ell\\!\\left(\\frac{X^{5/6}}{\\log X}\\right).\n \\label{eq:angular-cancellation}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-angular-hecke",
        "exact_quote": "Put $k=|\\ell|/2$. The complete primitive $L$-function has completion $$\\label{eq:angular-hecke-fe}\n \\Lambda(z,\\Psi)=\\left(\\frac{\\sqrt{3D}}{2\\pi}\\right)^z\n       \\Gamma(z+k)L(z,\\Psi),\\qquad\n \\Lambda(z,\\Psi)=\\epsilon_\\Psi\\Lambda(1-z,\\overline\\Psi),\n \\quad |\\epsilon_\\Psi|=1.$$ If $\\ell\\ne0$, or if $\\ell=0$ and $\\chi$ is nonprincipal cubic, the completed function is entire of order one.         "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, put $R=Nr$, and let $W$ have fixed compact support in $(0,\\infty)$ and uniformly bounded derivatives. For fixed $\\ell$, define $$\\mathcal C_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{d,u\\\\(d,r)=1}}|d|G(ru)\n       \\theta_\\ell(rud^3)N(ud^3)^{it}\n       W\\left(\\frac{N(ud^3)}V\\right).$$ Suppose $T\\ge2$, $V\\gg1$, and $R,V,T$ are bounded by fixed powers of $X$. Write $$M_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)=\n \\boldsymbol1_{\\ell=0}A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6+it)V^{5/6+it}\n                   \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}.$$ Then $$\\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\n       |\\mathcal C_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)-M_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)|\n           \\frac{dt}T\n \\ll_{\\ell,\\epsilon}X^\\epsilon\\sqrt V\\,R^{1/4}\\sqrt T.\n \\label{eq:angular-completed-height}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-angular-typei",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$, and $(a_r)$ is divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$. If $W_r$ has common fixed compact support in $(0,\\infty)$ and each fixed derivative is bounded by a fixed logarithmic power uniformly in $r$, then $$\\sum_r a_r\\sum_u\\mathcal D_\\ell(ru)W_r(Nu/U)\n       \\ll_\\ell X^{5/6-1/100}.\n \\label{eq:angular-typeI-low}$$ For the high-height assertion suppose instead that the derivative bounds are fixed independently of $X$. For $2\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho}$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\\sum_r|a_r|\n \\left|\\sum_u G_\\ell(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\\right|\\frac{dt}T\n \\ll_{\\ell,\\epsilon}{}&\n       X^{1/2+\\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\boldsymbol1_{\\ell=0}\n       O_D(X^{5/6}L^C T^{-D}),\n \\label{eq:angular-typeI-high}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\rho$ is the fixed parameter of <a href=\"#eq:power-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:power-parameters\">[eq:power-parameters]</a> and $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-023.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-angular-typei",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$, and $(a_r)$ is divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$. For $2\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho}$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\\sum_r|a_r|\n \\left|\\sum_u G_\\ell(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\\right|\\frac{dt}T\n \\ll_{\\ell,\\epsilon}{}&\n       X^{1/2+\\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\boldsymbol1_{\\ell=0}\n       O_D(X^{5/6}L^C T^{-D}),\n \\label{eq:angular-typeI-high}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\rho$ is the fixed parameter of <a href=\"#eq:power-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:power-parameters\">[eq:power-parameters]</a> and $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Primary element** ([source](#eq:gauss-definition)): An element prime to $3$ is called primary when it is congruent to $1$ modulo $3$.
- **Normalized cubic Gauss sum and model constant** ([source](#eq:gauss-definition)): For primary $b$, let $\chi_b$ be the cubic residue symbol with denominator $b$, extended by zero on nonunits modulo $b$, and put $$\label{eq:gauss-definition} G(b)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(b)}}\sum_{v\bmod b}\chi_b(v) e\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(v/b)\bigr), \qquad c_* = \frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(2/3)}.$$
- **Ideal von Mangoldt function** ([source](#sec:background)): The ideal von Mangoldt function is $\Lambda(n)=\log N\pi$ when $n=\pi^k$ for a prime $\pi$ and $k\ge1$, and is zero otherwise.
- **Prime factor multiplicity** ([source](#sec:background)): We write $\Omega(n)$ for the number of prime factors counted with multiplicity.
- **Divisor-bounded sequence** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A sequence is *divisor-bounded* if its absolute value is at most $L^C\tau(n)^C$ for some fixed $C$.
- **Admissible prime convolution** ([source](#eq:prime-convolution)): Fix a positive constant $c$, a positive integer $k_0$, and fixed support and smoothness bounds. An admissible prime convolution at length $Y$ is $$\beta(b)=\mu^2(b) \sum_{b=\pi_1\cdots\pi_k} \prod_{j=1}^k w_j\bigl(N(\pi_j)/F_j\bigr), \qquad 1\le k\le k_0,\qquad \prod_{j=1}^k F_j\asymp Y, \label{eq:prime-convolution}$$ where the primes are primary, the tuple is ordered, every supported prime has norm at least $Y^c$, and the $w_j$ have compact support in fixed subintervals of $(0,\infty)$. Each fixed derivative is bounded by a fixed power of $\log Y$, uniformly over the family under consideration. These bounds imply fixed divisor bounds for all coefficients below.
- **Dispersion scales** ([source](#eq:dispersion-scales)): $$AB\asymp X,\qquad X^{0.32}\le B\ll X^{1/2},\qquad H=\frac{B^2}{A},\qquad \mathcal Q=ABH^{1/3},\qquad L=\log X. \label{eq:dispersion-scales}$$ All sums over letters denoting ideals use primary generators prime to $3$, unless a full lattice is expressly specified. Let $\beta_b$ be divisor-bounded, supported on squarefree $b$ with $Nb\asymp B$. When $B>X^{0.40}$, suppose in addition that $\beta$ is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a fixed number of prime sequences of the kind in Proposition <a href="#prop:dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dual">10</a>, each prime having norm at least $X^c$ for some fixed $c>0$. The weights on the individual prime dyads are smooth; there is no additional cutoff coupling their products. Set $$\begin{aligned} \beta_b(u)&=\beta_b(Nb)^{iu},& S(u)&=\sum_b\beta_b(u)(Nb)^{-1/6},\\ F_u(a)&=\sum_b\beta_b(u)G(b)\overline{\chi_b(a)}. \end{aligned} \label{eq:dispersion-polynomials}$$ Let $W$ be a nonnegative radial smooth function, bounded in absolute value and supported in a fixed annulus in $\mathbb C$, and write $W_A(a)=W(a/\sqrt A)$.
- **Dispersion polynomials** ([source](#eq:dispersion-polynomials)): $$\begin{aligned} \beta_b(u)&=\beta_b(Nb)^{iu},& S(u)&=\sum_b\beta_b(u)(Nb)^{-1/6},\\ F_u(a)&=\sum_b\beta_b(u)G(b)\overline{\chi_b(a)}. \end{aligned} \label{eq:dispersion-polynomials}$$ Let $W$ be a nonnegative radial smooth function, bounded in absolute value and supported in a fixed annulus in $\mathbb C$, and write $W_A(a)=W(a/\sqrt A)$.
- **Rough prime weight** ([source](#pm-h-23)): Take a fixed smooth function $\psi$ with values in $[0,1]$, equal to $1$ on $(0,1]$ and to $0$ on $[2,\infty)$, and put $$\psi_y(\pi)=\psi(N(\pi)/y),\qquad \mathcal F_y(n)=\prod_{\pi\mid n}(1-\psi_y(\pi)).$$
- **Surrogate product** ([source](#pm-h-24)): If a prime lies in a bin of lower endpoint $\ell$, replace its norm by $\ell$ when forming a *surrogate product*, denoted $S$.
- **Angular kernels** ([source](#sec:angular)): For nonzero $z\in\mathbb C$, put $$\theta_\ell(z)=(z/\|z\|)^\ell,\qquad G_\ell(n)=\theta_\ell(n)G(n),\qquad \mathcal D_\ell(n)=\theta_\ell(n) \bigl(G(n)-c_*\mu^2(n)N(n)^{-1/6}\bigr).$$
- **Primary-generator angular Hecke character** ([source](#pm-h-34)): For an ideal $\mathfrak a$ prime to $3$, define $\Theta_\ell(\mathfrak a)=\theta_\ell(a)$, where $a$ is its primary generator.
- **Angular prime convolution** ([source](#pm-h-36)): Call $\beta_\ell(b)=\Theta_\ell(b)\beta_0(b)$ an angular prime convolution if $\beta_0$ is exactly an admissible convolution in <a href="#eq:prime-convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prime-convolution">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-model-constant | Model constant | = | $\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(2/3)}$ | Fixed normalization constant. |
| q-primary-main-coefficient | Coefficient of the primary-prime first-moment scale | = | $\frac65c_*$ | Sharp cutoff as the norm bound tends to infinity. |
| q-rational-half-sum-coefficient | Coefficient of the rational-prime half-sum scale | = | $\frac35c_*$ | Split rational primes; the remaining primes are treated separately in the source. |
| q-level-main-constant | Completed level-sum main-term constant | = | $(2\pi)^{5/3}/(3^{7/2}\Gamma(2/3))$ | Primary squarefree level and smooth compactly supported weight. |
| q-type-i-saving | Permitted fixed power-saving exponent in the Type I comparison | = | $1/100$ | The outer length and derivative regime stated in the proposition. |
| q-exceptional-twist-height | Norm-twist height including its unit offset | ≤ | $Y^{0.36}$ | Only the conductor-length neighborhoods specified in the proposition. |
| q-dispersion-frequency-scale | Dual frequency scale | = | $\frac{B^2}{A}$ | Norm lengths in the dispersion setup. |
| q-dispersion-benchmark | Dispersion benchmark | = | $ABH^{1/3}$ | The frequency aspect is defined from the bilinear norm lengths. |
| q-surrogate-product-upper-bound | Exact norm of a selected subproduct | ≤ | $X^{\kappa/4}S(m')$ | The bin ratio is fixed sufficiently small in terms of the decomposition parameter. |
| q-kappa-order | Decomposition power parameter | < | $\frac\gamma{100}$ | Positive parameters fixed before the logarithmic choices. |
| q-rho-order | Excess truncation-height parameter | < | $\frac\kappa{100}$ | Positive parameters fixed before the height assembly. |
| q-sharp-cutoff-height | Maximal Fourier truncation height | = | $X^{1/6+\rho}$ | The small loss in the truncation error is chosen below the positive excess-height parameter. |
| q-gauss-cube-identity | Cube of a normalized prime Gauss-sum value | = | $-\theta_1(\pi)$ | Prime modulus; negative powers are permitted by unit absolute value. |

        {
          "anchor": "eq:gauss-definition",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\omega=e^{2\\pi i/3}$ and $\\mathcal O=\\mathbb Z[\\omega]$. Write $N(z)=z\\bar z=|z|^2$, $e(x)=e^{2\\pi i x}$, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(z)=z+\\bar z$. An element prime to $3$ is called primary when it is congruent to $1$ modulo $3$. Every ideal prime to $3$ has exactly one primary generator. In particular, both conjugate prime ideals above a split rational prime are counted. For primary $b$, let $\\chi_b$ be the cubic residue symbol with denominator $b$, extended by zero on nonunits modulo $b$, and put $$\\label{eq:gauss-definition}\n G(b)=\\frac{1}{\\sqrt{N(b)}}\\sum_{v\\bmod b}\\chi_b(v)\n           e\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(v/b)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c_* = \\frac{(2\\pi)^{2/3}}{3\\Gamma(2/3)}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "eq:rational-main",
          "exact_quote": "The exact conversion is $$\\label{eq:pairing}\n G(\\pi)+G(\\bar\\pi)=\\frac{S_p}{\\sqrt p},\n \\qquad p=\\pi\\bar\\pi\\equiv1\\pmod3.$$ Indeed cubic reciprocity and conjugation give $$\\chi_\\pi(\\bar\\pi)=\\chi_{\\bar\\pi}(\\pi)\n     =\\overline{\\chi_\\pi(\\bar\\pi)},$$ so this cube root of unity is $1$. Via the isomorphism $\\mathbb Z/p\\mathbb Z\\simeq\\mathcal O/(\\pi)$, the additive coefficient in $e(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(v/\\pi))$ is $\\bar\\pi\\bmod\\pi$. It can therefore be removed without changing the cubic Gauss sum. Counting cube roots in $\\mathbb F_p$ gives the sum of the two conjugate cubic Gauss sums, proving <a href=\"#eq:pairing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pairing\">[eq:pairing]</a>. The conjugacy assertion also follows directly from the definition, since $\\chi_\\pi(-1)=1$. Inert primes have norm $p^2$ and contribute $O(\\sqrt X)$ in total. Consequently Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> gives $$\\label{eq:rational-main}\n \\sum_{\\substack{p\\le X\\\\p\\equiv1\\pmod3}}\n       \\frac{S_p}{2\\sqrt p}\n\\sim \\frac35c_*\\frac{X^{5/6}}{\\log X}\n =\\frac{(2\\pi)^{2/3}}{5\\Gamma(2/3)}\\frac{X^{5/6}}{\\log X}.$$ This is precisely the coefficient in [[Wan2021](#ref-Wan2021), Conjecture 4.13]. That formulation sums over all rational primes; the primes $p\\equiv2\\pmod3$ have $S_p=0$ because cubing permutes $\\mathbb F_p$, and $p=3$ does not affect the asymptotic.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
