        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-voronoi-background",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, and let $W$ be smooth and compactly supported in $(0,\\infty)$. Put $R=Nr$ and $A_0=(2\\pi)^{5/3}/(3^{7/2}\\Gamma(2/3))$. The completed sum $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{d,u\\\\(d,r)=1}}|d|G(ur)\n             W\\left(\\frac{N(ud^3)}{V}\\right)$$ has main term $A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\varphi(r)R^{-7/6}$. Its remaining term is $$\\frac{R^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\\pi)^2}\n \\sum_{\\substack{0\\ne\\nu\\in\\lambda^{-1}\\mathcal O, d\\\\(d,r)=1}}\n \\frac{a_r(\\nu)b_r(\\nu)}{N\\nu\\,(Nd)^{5/2}}\n \\check W\\left(\\frac{(2\\pi)^4N(d^3\\nu)V}{R^2}\\right),$$ where, for sufficiently small $\\sigma>0$, $$\\check W(v)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\\int_{(-\\sigma)}v^z\n \\frac{\\Gamma(5/6-z)\\Gamma(7/6-z)}\n      {\\Gamma(z-1/6)\\Gamma(z+1/6)}\\mathcal MW(z)\\,dz.$$ The coefficients depend only on $r$ and $\\nu$, independently of $V$ and $W$. They vanish outside representations $$\\nu=\\lambda^j\\zeta h w(h')^3,\\quad j\\ge-1,\\quad\n h,h'\\mid r^\\infty,\\quad (w,r)=1,\\quad \\mu^2(hw)=1,$$ where $h,h',w$ are primary and $\\zeta$ is a unit. On this support, they are $$a_r(\\nu)=a^\\star(\\lambda^{-3}\\nu),\\qquad\n b_r(\\nu)=\\mu\\left(\\frac{r}{(\\lambda\\nu,r)}\\right)\n          \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{\\varphi\\left(r/(\\lambda\\nu,r)\\right)},$$ where $a^\\star$ is the theta coefficient in [[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (5.74)]; the second formula is its Equation (5.68). The index $\\lambda^{-3}\\nu$ is the one supplied by its coefficient identity (5.79). They satisfy $$|a_r(\\nu)|\\ll 3^{\\max(j,0)/3}|h'|,\n \\qquad |b_r(\\nu)|\\le N((\\lambda\\nu,r)).$$ In particular, if $V,V^{-1},R\\ll X^{C_0}$ for fixed $C_0$, $W$ is supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, and each fixed derivative bound is at most a fixed logarithmic power, then $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}\n +O_\\epsilon(X^\\epsilon R^{1/2}).\n \\label{eq:voronoi}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\delta,\\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href=\"#eq:prime-convolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prime-convolution\">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Let $v,e\\in\\mathcal O\\setminus\\{0\\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\\le Y^\\delta$, and let $1+|u|\\le Y^{0.36}$. Put $$A(p,q)=\\sum_{(b,e)=1}\\beta(b)N(b)^{iu}\\chi_b(vpq^2).$$ If $p,q$ run through coprime primary squarefree elements of respective norm lengths $P,Q\\ge1$, and either $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-1\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}\\le\\delta,$$ or $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,$$ then $$\\sum_{p,q}|A(p,q)|^2\\ll Y^{7/3-\\eta}.\n \\label{eq:exceptional-moment}$$ The implied constant is uniform for the specified support and smoothness bounds.",

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cubic-sieve-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-cls) / [proof](#pm-h-8): Import the cubic large sieve with both squarefree hypotheses retained. -> Cubic character second-moment bound.
- **step-ordinary-character-sieve** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve): Expand primitive Gauss sums, use character orthogonality and bound the planar additive Gram matrix by separation and packing. -> Ordinary character sieve without a small-power conductor loss.
- **step-cubic-sieve-extensions** [claim](#stmt-lem-cubic-extensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cubic-extensions): Factor arguments into squarefree, squared-squarefree and cube parts, then apply the squarefree sieve with the appropriate triangle or fixing argument. -> Separate unrestricted-inner and unrestricted-outer sieve bounds.
- **step-level-voronoi-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-voronoi-background) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-voronoi-background): Use the unconditional level formula with its precise coefficient index, then sum the dual terms absolutely on a left Mellin line. -> Completed level main term and uniform remainder.
- **step-metaplectic-height-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-hb-height) / [proof](#stmt-thm-hb-height): Combine the imported metaplectic mean square with continuation, residue and strip-growth information from the stated series identities. -> Height mean square and analytic contour-shift prerequisites.
- **step-type-i-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-typei) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-typei): Invert cube completion, identify the model by squarefree lattice counting, and estimate the long completion tail by counting or the cubic sieve. -> Power-saving Type I comparison with the squarefree model.
- **step-type-i-height** [claim](#stmt-prop-typeiheight) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-typeiheight): Shift Mellin inversion past the residue and apply Minkowski and Cauchy to the metaplectic height mean square. -> Height-average Type I bound with a decaying residue contribution.
- **step-smooth-character-dual** [claim](#stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality): Apply the primitive functional equation on a left contour, truncate dual dyads and return each finite dyad to the symmetry line. -> Short dual dyads with rowwise scalar phases and a common integrable majorant.
- **step-off-diagonal-gram** [claim](#stmt-lem-character-gram) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-character-gram): Separate common divisors, use Poisson summation with vanishing zero frequency and apply the unrestricted-inner sieve to separated frequency coefficients. -> Squared off-diagonal Gram row bound.
- **step-exceptional-moments** [claim](#stmt-prop-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-character-gram-2): Discard prime-power discrepancies, apply a short convolution identity, shorten dominant full factors and use repeated-factor moments followed by the Gram bound in a limiting-exponent contradiction. -> Fixed neighborhood and power saving at the exceptional configurations.
- **step-corrected-dispersion** [claim](#stmt-prop-dispersion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dispersion): Open the second moment, separate Poisson frequencies, bound noncubes and retain the cube main term. Match the mixed term by Type I comparison; use height averaging to retain the exact diagonal. -> Coarse, corrected, short and height-averaged dispersion estimates.
- **step-prime-detector** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime-detector): Use roughness to exclude longer prime products on the envelope and expand each prime factor independently into the distinguished or remaining part. -> Exact prime detector and bounded distinguished-prime tuples.
- **step-prime-grouping** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-grouping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime-grouping): Analyze subset sums of prime-scale exponents and use compactness away from the exceptional balanced partition. -> Admissible bilinear scales and an isolated prime transition.
- **step-stopping-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-stopping-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stopping-separation): Fix the final bin and selected and remaining endpoint counts; apply the reciprocal binomial selection weight and allocate all predicates to the selected side. -> Exact independent bilinear coefficients with controlled index count.
- **step-ordinary-sequence-condition** [claim](#stmt-lem-decomposition-sw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-decomposition-sw): Discard smooth supports by Rankin's inequality, fix a largest prime and its complementary factors, and apply prime Siegel–Walfisz cancellation on the resulting norm intervals. -> Sequence cancellation with the permitted exclusions and norm twists.
- **step-exact-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-prop-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-decomposition-sw-2): Assemble the detector, grouping and stopped branches; bound sparse late stops and count the prime-transition pieces before estimating either kernel. -> Exact decomposition matching each dispersion regime with noncircular parameter choices.
- **step-low-height-bilinear** [claim](#stmt-prop-bilinear-low) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bilinear-low): Apply Cauchy to corrected dispersion, restore cross-coprimality using roughness and use the uncorrected estimate with prime density at the transition. -> Arbitrary logarithmic comparison saving and the separate transition bound.
- **step-integrated-bilinear** [claim](#stmt-prop-bilinear-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bilinear-height): Use averaged dispersion directly at larger shorter-length scales; otherwise localize both variables, exploit bounded near-cell partners and integrate by parts for far pairs. -> Integrated Gauss-sum bilinear estimate without roughness or sequence conditions.
- **step-fourier-truncation** [claim](#stmt-lem-perron-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perron-truncation): Convolve the logarithmic interval indicator with a Schwartz approximate identity and sum endpoint decay over integer norms. -> Smooth truncated indicator and aggregate endpoint error.
- **step-sharp-prime-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-perron-truncation-2): Assemble low and high height blocks, remove the undecomposed model at high heights, sum the prime transition, and finish by dyadic summation and the prime ideal theorem. -> Sharp primary-prime first moment with the stated constant.
- **step-angular-hecke-inputs** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-hecke) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-hecke): Identify the fixed-infinity completion and conductor, exclude the exceptional real character and apply the prime estimate in the logarithmic conductor range. -> Fixed-angular functional equation, entireness and prime cancellation.
- **step-angular-completed-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-completed-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-completed-height): Use an ordinary mean value for short completed lengths; otherwise truncate the angular Voronoi dual sum and estimate its finite frequency dyads on the symmetry line. -> Pole-subtracted completed angular height average.
- **step-angular-type-i** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-typei) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-typei): Invert angular cube completion with its positive angular divisor phase, use zero-area angular lattice counting at low heights and completed height averages at high heights. -> Low and high height angular Type I estimates.
- **step-angular-exceptional-moments** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-dual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-dual): Extend full smooth duality to fixed infinity type, keep conductor phases rowwise and repeat the discrepancy, multiplicity and Gram arguments with the angular factor in common coefficients. -> Exceptional moment saving for each fixed angular mode.
- **step-angular-sequence-condition** [claim](#stmt-lem-angular-sw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-angular-sw): Repeat largest-prime selection and apply the fixed-angular prime estimate to the remaining prime while controlling exclusions absolutely. -> Sequence condition for the twisted coefficients themselves.
- **step-angular-dispersion** [claim](#stmt-prop-angular-dispersion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-dispersion): Retain radial Poisson weights, transfer general-coefficient bounds, use angular exceptional moments and twisted sequence cancellation, and match the cube main term through the original radial Type I comparison. -> Angular dispersion and bilinear bounds with unchanged absolute small-length parameter.
- **step-fixed-angular-prime-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angular-dispersion-2): Allocate angular phases multiplicatively through the exact decomposition, repeat the sharp-cutoff assembly and remove the nonzero-mode model by angular prime cancellation. -> Fixed-angular comparison and cancellation for each fixed nonzero mode.

          "exact_quote": "Let $r$ be primary and squarefree, and let $W$ be smooth and compactly supported in $(0,\\infty)$. Put $R=Nr$ and $A_0=(2\\pi)^{5/3}/(3^{7/2}\\Gamma(2/3))$. The completed sum $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{d,u\\\\(d,r)=1}}|d|G(ur)\n             W\\left(\\frac{N(ud^3)}{V}\\right)$$ has main term $A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\varphi(r)R^{-7/6}$. Its remaining term is $$\\frac{R^{1/2}}{3^{7/2}(2\\pi)^2}\n \\sum_{\\substack{0\\ne\\nu\\in\\lambda^{-1}\\mathcal O, d\\\\(d,r)=1}}\n \\frac{a_r(\\nu)b_r(\\nu)}{N\\nu\\,(Nd)^{5/2}}\n \\check W\\left(\\frac{(2\\pi)^4N(d^3\\nu)V}{R^2}\\right),$$ where, for sufficiently small $\\sigma>0$, $$\\check W(v)=\\frac{1}{2\\pi i}\\int_{(-\\sigma)}v^z\n \\frac{\\Gamma(5/6-z)\\Gamma(7/6-z)}\n      {\\Gamma(z-1/6)\\Gamma(z+1/6)}\\mathcal MW(z)\\,dz.$$ The coefficients depend only on $r$ and $\\nu$, independently of $V$ and $W$. They vanish outside representations $$\\nu=\\lambda^j\\zeta h w(h')^3,\\quad j\\ge-1,\\quad\n h,h'\\mid r^\\infty,\\quad (w,r)=1,\\quad \\mu^2(hw)=1,$$ where $h,h',w$ are primary and $\\zeta$ is a unit. On this support, they are $$a_r(\\nu)=a^\\star(\\lambda^{-3}\\nu),\\qquad\n b_r(\\nu)=\\mu\\left(\\frac{r}{(\\lambda\\nu,r)}\\right)\n          \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{\\varphi\\left(r/(\\lambda\\nu,r)\\right)},$$ where $a^\\star$ is the theta coefficient in [[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (5.74)]; the second formula is its Equation (5.68). The index $\\lambda^{-3}\\nu$ is the one supplied by its coefficient identity (5.79). They satisfy $$|a_r(\\nu)|\\ll 3^{\\max(j,0)/3}|h'|,\n \\qquad |b_r(\\nu)|\\le N((\\lambda\\nu,r)).$$ In particular, if $V,V^{-1},R\\ll X^{C_0}$ for fixed $C_0$, $W$ is supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, and each fixed derivative bound is at most a fixed logarithmic power, then $$\\mathcal C_r(V;W)=\n A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6)V^{5/6}\\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}\n +O_\\epsilon(X^\\epsilon R^{1/2}).\n \\label{eq:voronoi}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-hb-height",
          "exact_quote": "For primary squarefree $r$, the Dirichlet series $$f_r(s)=\\sum_u G(ru)(Nu)^{-s},\\qquad \\operatorname{Re}s>1,$$ continues meromorphically to $\\operatorname{Re}s>1/2$, with at most a simple pole at $5/6$. It has polynomial growth in closed strips there, away from its pole. For sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ and every $T\\ge1$, $$\\int_{-T}^T|f_r(1/2+\\epsilon+it)|^2dt\n \\ll_\\epsilon (Nr)^{1/2+4\\epsilon}T^2.\n \\label{eq:hb-mean-square}$$ Its residue $\\rho_r$ satisfies $|\\rho_r|\\ll (Nr)^{-1/6}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-typei",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$ and $1\\le R\\ll X^{.51}$. Let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded and supported on $Nr\\asymp R$. Let $W_r$ have support in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $L$, uniformly in $r$. Then $$\\sum_r a_r\\sum_u\n \\left(G(ru)-c_*\\frac{\\mu^2(ru)}{N(ru)^{1/6}}\\right)W_r(Nu/U)\n \\ll X^{5/6-\\eta}$$ for some fixed $\\eta>0$. One may take $\\eta=1/100$, with constants depending on the stated fixed parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Power-saving Type I comparison with the squarefree model.",

    },

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-typeiheight",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $RU\\asymp X$, and let $(a_r)$ be divisor-bounded on $Nr\\asymp R$. Suppose the smooth compactly supported weights $W_r$ have fixed support ratios and uniformly bounded derivatives, independently of $X$. For $T\\ge2$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\\sum_{Nr\\asymp R}|a_r|\n       \\left|\\sum_uG(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\\right|\\frac{dt}{T}\n \\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\\ll_\\epsilon\n       X^{1/2+\\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\n       +O_D\\bigl(X^{5/6}L^{C}T^{-D}\\bigr),\n \\label{eq:typeI-height}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\chi$ be a primitive nonprincipal Hecke character of trivial infinite parameter and conductor norm $D$. Let $W$ be smooth and supported in a fixed compact subinterval of $(0,\\infty)$, with each fixed derivative bounded by a fixed power of $\\log Y$. The full sum $\\sum_n W(N(n)/Z)\\chi(n)N(n)^{it}$ admits a decomposition into dual norm dyads $$J\\ll Y^\\epsilon D(1+|t|)^2/Z$$ with amplitude $O(Y^\\epsilon\\sqrt{Z/J})$. The coefficients in a dual dyad are divisor-bounded, and its character is $\\overline\\chi$. All additional norm shifts can be integrated against a common conductor-independent $L^1$ majorant of mass $O(Y^\\epsilon)$. The omitted tail is smaller than any prescribed negative power of $Y$, provided the lengths and heights are bounded by fixed powers of $Y$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-smooth-character-dual",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Apply the primitive functional equation on a left contour, truncate dual dyads and return each finite dyad to the symmetry line.",
      "output": "Short dual dyads with rowwise scalar phases and a common integrable majorant.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-smooth-character-duality"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-character-gram",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-character-gram",
          "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be primary squarefree with $N(p)\\asymp P$, and let $W$ be a fixed smooth radial norm weight of compact support in $(0,\\infty)$. For primary squarefree $p'$ of the same norm length put $$T_{p,p'}=\\sum_{n\\equiv1\\, (3)}\n     W(N(n)/Z)\\chi_p(n)\\overline{\\chi_{p'}(n)}.$$ For $Z\\ge1$ and every $\\epsilon>0$, $$\\sum_{p'\\ne p}|T_{p,p'}|^2\n \\ll_\\epsilon (PZ)^\\epsilon\n      Z\\left(P+(P^3/Z)^{2/3}\\right).\n \\label{eq:near-orthogonality}$$ The same bound holds with a subset of the available $p'$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-off-diagonal-gram",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Separate common divisors, use Poisson summation with vanishing zero frequency and apply the unrestricted-inner sieve to separated frequency coefficients.",

    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dual",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\delta,\\eta>0$, depending only on the fixed data in <a href=\"#eq:prime-convolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:prime-convolution\">[eq:prime-convolution]</a>, with the following property. Let $v,e\\in\\mathcal O\\setminus\\{0\\}$ satisfy $N(v),N(e)\\le Y^\\delta$, and let $1+|u|\\le Y^{0.36}$. Put $$A(p,q)=\\sum_{(b,e)=1}\\beta(b)N(b)^{iu}\\chi_b(vpq^2).$$ If $p,q$ run through coprime primary squarefree elements of respective norm lengths $P,Q\\ge1$, and either $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-1\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0\\le\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}\\le\\delta,$$ or $$\\left|\\frac{\\log P}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\frac{\\log Q}{\\log Y}-\\frac13\\right|\\le\\delta,$$ then $$\\sum_{p,q}|A(p,q)|^2\\ll Y^{7/3-\\eta}.\n \\label{eq:exceptional-moment}$$ The implied constant is uniform for the specified support and smoothness bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-dispersion",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-exceptional-moments",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-dispersion",
          "exact_quote": "Under the preceding hypotheses the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *If $H\\gg1$ and $|u|\\le X^{0.17}$, and the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$, then $$\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_u(a)|^2\\ll \\mathcal Q L^{O(1)}.$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *Suppose $|u|\\le L^{O(1)}$, $H\\gg1$, and $\\beta$ has property *(SW)* and is supported on elements all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $C_r,C_g$ are sufficiently large, then for $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_a\\mu^2(a)W_A(a)\n     \\left|F_u(a)-c_*\\overline{G(a)}(Na)^{-1/6}S(u)\\right|^2\n     \\ll \\mathcal Q L^{-K}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-corrected}$$ For $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$ one has the uncorrected estimate $$\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_u(a)|^2\n     \\ll A\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\n     \\mathcal Q\\left\\{\\left(B^{-1}\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\right)^2+L^{-K}\\right\\}.\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-short}$$ Here $1\\ll H$ means only that $H$ is bounded below by a positive constant. In these low-height assertions the derivatives of $W$ are bounded independently of $X$.*\n\n\n3.  *Suppose $u=\\pm t+u_0$, $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, $2T+|u_0|\\le X^{0.17}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j L^{Cj}$ for fixed $C$. For every fixed $K>0$, if $T\\ge L^{C_t}$ with $C_t$ sufficiently large, then $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q L^{-K}),\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-height}$$ where the exact diagonal is $$\\mathcal D_W=\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2\n                      \\sum_{(a,b)=1}W_A(a).$$ No lower bound on $H$ is required here. When $B\\le X^{0.40}$ the restriction on $u_0$ can be omitted.*\n\n\n    *There is also an absolute sufficiently small $\\gamma>0$ such that, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, $T\\gg X^{0.01}$, and $|\\partial^jW|\\ll_j X^{j\\gamma}$, the corresponding estimate is $$\\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\sum_a W_A(a)|F_{\\pm t+u_0}(a)|^2\\frac{dt}{T}\n     \\le \\int_{T\\le|t|\\le2T}\\mathcal D_W\\frac{dt}{T}\n           +O(\\mathcal Q X^{-10\\gamma}).\n     \\label{eq:dispersion-power}$$ This $\\gamma$ is independent of the fixed powers in the divisor bounds. In the last two estimates, a radial support of width $O(J^{-1})$ gives $$\\mathcal D_W\\ll \\frac AJ\\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2$$ for $J\\le L^{O(1)}$, or $J\\le X^\\gamma$, respectively. In particular, when $B\\le X^{0.40}$, the coefficients may be restricted sharply to any norm cell without changing these assertions.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-corrected-dispersion",
      "inputs": [
        "Structured exceptional moments",
        "Sieve bounds",
        "Type I comparison",
        "Regime-specific sequence and roughness assumptions"

      "method": "Open the second moment, separate Poisson frequencies, bound noncubes and retain the cube main term. Match the mixed term by Type I comparison; use height averaging to retain the exact diagonal.",
      "output": "Coarse, corrected, short and height-averaged dispersion estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-dispersion"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-detector",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-detector",
          "exact_quote": "For squarefree $n$ on the envelope support and sufficiently large $X$, $$\\label{eq:prime-detector}\n \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{n\\ {\\mathrm{prime}}\\}}\n =\\mathcal F_z(n)-\n  \\boldsymbol 1_{\\{\\Omega(n)=2\\}}\\mathcal F_z(n).$$ Moreover, $\\mathcal F_z(n)$ is an exact sum of bounded-length distinguished prime tuples $$\\label{eq:distinguished-tuples}\n \\mathcal F_z(n)=\n \\sum_{i\\ge0}\\frac1{i!}\n \\sum_{n=\\pi_1\\cdots\\pi_i h}\n \\mathcal F_w(h)\\prod_{j=1}^i\n       (\\psi_w(\\pi_j)-\\psi_z(\\pi_j)).$$ Only boundedly many $i$ contribute, and the distinguished primes have norms in $[w,2z]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-decomposition-sw",
          "exact_quote": "Every nonsparse ordinary $b$ coefficient satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(SW)</span>: against any nonprincipal cubic Hecke character of the indicated trivial infinite parameter and primitive conductor of fixed polylogarithmic norm, its sum is $O_D(BL^{-D})$ for every fixed $D$.     {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-decomposition",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-prime-grouping",
        "step-stopping-separation",
        "step-ordinary-sequence-condition"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-decomposition",
          "exact_quote": "For either kind of height block, the prime sum with envelope $V$ has an exact decomposition into a number of Type I and bilinear terms bounded by a fixed power of $L$. This decomposition is valid with either kernel $$G(n)N(n)^{it}\n \\quad\\hbox{or}\\quad\n c_*\\mu^2(n)N(n)^{-1/6+it},$$ and uses the same coefficients for the two kernels. The coefficients are independent of $t$, divisor-bounded, and have all fixed absolute moments of the usual size $Y L^{O(1)}$ on a norm dyad of length $Y$. The resulting terms have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Type I terms have an unrestricted inner variable $u$ and an outer variable $r$ with $$N(r)\\asymp R,\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{.40}\\quad\\hbox{on ordinary blocks},\\qquad\n     R\\le X^{1/3-\\kappa/2}\\quad\\hbox{on upper blocks}.$$ Their inner weight is a smooth envelope at length $X/R$, with fixed derivative bounds, depending permissibly on $r$.*\n\n2.  *Bilinear terms have independent coefficients $\\alpha_a,\\beta_b$, may be restricted individually to squarefree $a,b$, and satisfy $$N(a)\\asymp A,\\quad N(b)\\asymp B,\\quad AB\\asymp X,\\quad B\\ll\\sqrt X.$$ On ordinary blocks, $B\\ge cX^{1/3}$ for some fixed $c>0$; on upper blocks, $B\\ge X^{1/3-3\\kappa}$. Whenever $B>X^{.40}$, the $b$ coefficient is a squarefree-restricted convolution of a bounded number of prime sequences on independent smooth norm dyads, each prime having norm at least a fixed positive power of $X$. There is no additional cutoff coupling those prime dyads.*\n\n3.  *On ordinary blocks, apart from the sparse terms in part (5), the $b$ coefficient is supported on numbers all of whose prime factors have norm greater than $L^{C_r}$ and satisfies the property <span class=\"upright\">(SW)</span> required in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. Here $C_r$ is any fixed constant chosen sufficiently large later.*\n\n\n4.  *For any fixed $C_g$, the ordinary bilinear terms with $$1\\ll H:=B^2/A<L^{C_g}$$ consist of precisely three primes: one prime on the $b$ side and two specified prime dyads on the $a$ side. All three scales are $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, their weights and multiplicities are bounded, and the number of these terms is $O((1+\\log L)^{O(1)})$. In each such term, $$\\label{eq:decomposition-triple-moments}\n     \\sum_a|\\alpha_a|^2\\ll A/L^2,\n     \\qquad\n     \\sum_b|\\beta_b|^2+\\sum_b|\\beta_b|\\ll B/L.$$ All other nonsparse ordinary bilinear terms have $H\\ge L^{C_g}$ for sufficiently large $X$.*\n\n5.  *The remaining ordinary terms have $X^{.35}\\ll B<X^{.40}$ and a sparse $b$ support. Their total contribution, for either kernel and uniformly in $t$, is $O(X^{5/6-\\eta})$ for some $\\eta>0$ depending on the fixed parameters.*\n\n*The exponents in the number of terms and in the divisor bounds can be chosen independently of $C_r$ and $C_g$. All asserted identities hold before estimating either kernel.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Exact decomposition matching each dispersion regime with noncircular parameter choices.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-decomposition-sw-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-low",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-corrected-dispersion",
        "step-exact-decomposition"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-low",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $X^{.32}\\le B\\ll\\sqrt X$, $H\\gg1$, and the coefficient $\\beta_b$ satisfies the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>. At low heights assume, in addition, its roughness and Siegel–Walfisz hypotheses. For every fixed $D,U>0$, the logarithmic parameters in that proposition can be chosen so that, uniformly for $|t|\\le L^U$ and $H\\ge L^{C_g}$, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_b\\mathcal D(ab)N(ab)^{it}\n                 V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-low}$$ There is also the following transition estimate. Suppose $b$ is one prime, $a$ is a product of two primes, each of the three primes has its own specified smooth dyad of length $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, and their weights are bounded. If $1\\ll H<L^{C_g}$, then the left side of <a href=\"#eq:bilinear-low\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:bilinear-low\">[eq:bilinear-low]</a> is $$O\\!\\left(X^{5/6}L^{-3/2}\\right).\n \\label{eq:bilinear-transition}$$ The constants are uniform for the families of dyads in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:decomposition\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:decomposition\">15</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-low-height-bilinear",
      "inputs": [
        "Corrected and short dispersion",
        "Independent bilinear coefficients",
        "Prime-transition coefficient moments"
      ],
      "method": "Apply Cauchy to corrected dispersion, restore cross-coprimality using roughness and use the uncorrected estimate with prime density at the transition.",
      "output": "Arbitrary logarithmic comparison saving and the separate transition bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-bilinear-low"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-height",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-corrected-dispersion"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bilinear-height",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X_0\\asymp X$, and let $h_T$ be smooth, supported where $T\\le |t|\\le2T$, with $h_T^{(j)}(t)\\ll_jT^{-j}$. Suppose $$L^{C_t}\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho},\\qquad B\\ll\\sqrt X,$$ and suppose that either $$\\begin{aligned}\n T\\ll X^{.01},&\\qquad cX^{1/3}\\le B,\\quad c>0\\text{ fixed},\\tag{a}\\\\\n T\\gg X^{.01},&\\qquad X^{1/3-3\\kappa}\\le B.\\tag{b}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Assume the structural hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:dispersion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:dispersion\">13</a>, but impose neither roughness nor Siegel–Walfisz conditions. For every fixed $D>0$, by taking $C_t$ sufficiently large, $$\\sum_{a,b}\\alpha_a\\beta_bG(ab)V\\!\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}X\\right)\n \\int h_T(t)\\left(\\frac{N(ab)}{X_0}\\right)^{it}\\frac{dt}{T}\n       \\ll X^{5/6}L^{-D}.\n \\label{eq:bilinear-integrated}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-integrated-bilinear",
      "inputs": [
        "Height-averaged dispersion",
        "Fixed smooth product envelope",
        "Ordered power and logarithmic parameters"
      ],
      "method": "Use averaged dispersion directly at larger shorter-length scales; otherwise localize both variables, exploit bounded near-cell partners and integrate by parts for far pairs.",
      "output": "Integrated Gauss-sum bilinear estimate without roughness or sequence conditions.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-bilinear-height"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-perron-truncation",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-perron-truncation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $1\\le T\\le X$, and let $V$ be a fixed smooth function supported in $(1/2,4)$, equal to one on a neighborhood of $[1,2]$. There is a smooth function $I_T$ such that $$I_T(s)=\\frac1{2\\pi}\\int_{\\mathbb R}\n       \\eta(t/T)\\frac{1-2^{-it}}{it}e^{its}\\,dt,\n \\label{eq:interval-fourier}$$ where $\\eta$ is a fixed smooth cutoff supported in $[-1,1]$ and equal to one near zero. For every $m>0$, $$\\left|1_{[1,2]}(v)-V(v)I_T(\\log v)\\right|\n \\ll_m \\sum_{b\\in\\{0,\\log2\\}}\n             (1+T|\\log v-b|)^{-m}\n \\label{eq:interval-boundary-error}$$ on the support of $V$, and the difference is zero outside it. If coefficients collected at each integer norm satisfy $|c_n|\\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon$ for $X/2<n<4X$, then $$\\sum_n |c_n|\\left|1_{[X,2X]}(n)\n            -V(n/X)I_T(\\log(n/X))\\right|\n       \\ll_\\epsilon X^\\epsilon(1+X/T).\n \\label{eq:perron-error}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-023.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-fourier-truncation",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed interval envelope",
        "Equal-norm coefficient bound"
      ],
      "method": "Convolve the logarithmic interval indicator with a Schwartz approximate identity and sum endpoint decay over integer norms.",

In these cases, for every fixed $A,M>0$, $$\\label{eq:angular-prime-sw}\n \\sum_{N\\mathfrak p\\le x}\\Psi(\\mathfrak p)\\log N\\mathfrak p\n       \\ll_{\\ell,A,M}x(\\log x)^{-M},\n \\qquad N\\mathfrak f_\\chi\\le(\\log x)^A.$$ Ramified primes have character value zero. Write $$M_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)=\n \\boldsymbol1_{\\ell=0}A_0\\mathcal MW(5/6+it)V^{5/6+it}\n                   \\frac{\\varphi(r)}{R^{7/6}}.$$ Then $$\\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\n       |\\mathcal C_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)-M_{r,\\ell,t}(V;W)|\n           \\frac{dt}T\n \\ll_{\\ell,\\epsilon}X^\\epsilon\\sqrt V\\,R^{1/4}\\sqrt T.\n \\label{eq:angular-completed-height}$$",

For $2\\le T\\ll X^{1/6+\\rho}$ and every fixed $D>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\int_{|t|\\asymp T}\\sum_r|a_r|\n \\left|\\sum_u G_\\ell(ru)(Nu)^{it}W_r(Nu/U)\\right|\\frac{dt}T\n \\ll_{\\ell,\\epsilon}{}&\n       X^{1/2+\\epsilon}R^{3/4}T^{1/2}\\notag\\\\\n &+\\boldsymbol1_{\\ell=0}\n       O_D(X^{5/6}L^C T^{-D}),\n \\label{eq:angular-typeI-high}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\rho$ is the fixed parameter of <a href=\"#eq:power-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:power-parameters\">[eq:power-parameters]</a> and $C$ depends only on the coefficient bounds.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-primary-prime-first-moment**: The sharp-cutoff first moment has the stated positive main term when summed over all primary primes, including both conjugate prime ideals above a split rational prime.
- **invariant-fixed-angular-comparison**: The comparison with the elementary model holds for each fixed integer angular mode; each fixed nonzero mode has cancellation at the first-moment scale.
- **invariant-exact-independent-decomposition**: The prime decomposition is exact for both stated kernels and uses the same coefficients. The bilinear coefficients are independent, and the required prime-convolution structure is retained in its stipulated range.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-rational-normalization**: Transfer the primary-prime main coefficient directly to the rational-prime half-sum. -> The conjugate-pair conversion changes the coefficient, and the historical displays cited in the paper have conflicting normalizations.
- **anti-use-growing-angular-mode**: Use the angular theorem for modes growing with the norm cutoff, including progressively narrower angular localization. -> Pointwise estimates for fixed modes provide no asserted uniform control as the mode grows.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-coefficient-improvement**: Apply the exceptional-moment saving to arbitrary divisor-bounded coefficients or introduce a sharp condition coupling prime factors. -> The additional cancellation uses independently weighted prime convolutions; an arbitrary sequence need not retain that structure.
- **anti-use-discard-cube-frequencies**: Treat every nonzero Poisson frequency as an error, including cube frequencies. -> Cube frequencies contribute the model main term required for cancellation in the corrected square.
- **anti-use-complementary-cubic-powers**: Claim that the fixed-power consequence settles the complementary conjecture for nonzero multiples of three. -> Those powers reduce to prime-angle sums for which the Hecke estimates used here do not give the required stronger scale.
- **anti-use-finite-cutoff-guarantee**: Read the asymptotic as a certified error tolerance at a specified finite cutoff or as an execution-time guarantee. -> The main statement supplies a little-oh remainder without a numerical threshold or a computational cost contract.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal first-moment and angular conclusions concern asymptotic prime sums, rather than a finite input-output construction. The exceptional saving also uses a limiting argument: “We use a limiting-exponent contradiction, and give the uniformity argument explicitly at the end.” Finite checks of the exact decomposition would certify only that subsidiary identity on the checked inputs, not the asymptotic conclusions or the uniform existence of the saving constants.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sharp prime-sum asymptotic supplies no finite error threshold or software execution contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cancellation for fixed angular modes does not construct a sampling procedure or a simulation of prime directions. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gauss-sum definition specifies a mathematical target, but the paper provides no evaluator with certified numerical precision or termination bounds. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The stopping-bin construction separates a finite factorization sum into independent coefficient sums. Fixing the last bin and its selected and remaining counts permits inverse-binomial weighting to recover the original coefficient exactly. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Collecting coefficients by rational norm establishes divisor-moment estimates, but does not specify storage operations, indexing guarantees or a data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ordinary mean-value inequality controls an analytic height integral and provides no accelerator mapping, memory-access scheme or hardware throughput guarantee. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive first-moment bias qualifies the interpretation of unit-circle equidistribution, but supplies neither a cryptographic construction nor a security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Gram estimate concerns cubic-character correlations under a radial norm weight; it does not construct a learning model, training method or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The exceptional-moment proof obtains uniform neighborhoods and savings through a limiting contradiction, rather than solving a finite optimisation problem or producing numerical parameter values. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independence of the decomposition coefficients is algebraic separation of summation variables; it does not construct distributed execution, synchronization or fault recovery. |
| other (analytic number theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The exact prime decomposition constructs coefficient classes compatible with the low-height comparison and high-height estimates. It is an adaptable proof construction for structured first-moment problems when the required analytic inputs and coefficient conditions can be established. |

All three scales are $X^{1/3}L^{O(1)}$, their weights and multiplicities are bounded, and the number of these terms is $O((1+\\log L)^{O(1)})$. Their total contribution, for either kernel and uniformly in $t$, is $O(X^{5/6-\\eta})$ for some $\\eta>0$ depending on the fixed parameters.*\n\n*The exponents in the number of terms and in the divisor bounds can be chosen independently of $C_r$ and $C_g$. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-primary-prime-normalization** (hypothesis): The main asymptotic uses the prescribed normalized cubic Gauss sum and counts all primary prime generators, including conjugate prime ideals above split rational primes. Blocked use: ['Applying the primary-prime coefficient directly to a rational-prime half-sum.', 'Changing the additive character or Gauss-sum normalization while retaining the same constant.']
- **limit-historical-coefficient-identification** (formal_scope): The source distinguishes conflicting printed historical coefficients and identifies precisely the all-primary-prime formulation it proves. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the conflicting rational-prime and primary-prime displays are equivalent.', 'Claiming that the paper determines which conflicting historical coefficient is authoritative.']
- **limit-asymptotic-error-threshold** (approximation): The prime first moment is an asymptotic statement with a little-oh remainder; it supplies no explicit numerical cutoff for a requested finite error tolerance. Blocked use: ['Certifying a numerical approximation at a specified finite cutoff solely from the main theorem.', 'Deriving an execution-time guarantee from the first-moment scale.']
- **limit-squarefree-sieve-regimes** (hypothesis): The original cubic sieve requires squarefree support in both arguments. Removing either restriction requires its corresponding extension, with the distinct bound and coefficient hypotheses retained. Blocked use: ['Dropping squarefree support while keeping the original sieve bound unchanged.', 'Using the unrestricted-inner extension for arbitrary coefficients without its divisor bounds.']
- **limit-primitive-character-sieve** (hypothesis): The ordinary character sieve concerns distinct primitive ray characters with trivial infinite parameter and a bounded ramified conductor part. Blocked use: ['Counting repeated inducing characters as distinct primitive rows.', 'Allowing an uncontrolled conductor component above the ramified prime while retaining the stated uniform constant.']
- **limit-level-completion-and-smoothness** (hypothesis): The level formula evaluates a cube-completed sum at a primary squarefree level. Its stated remainder requires polynomial scale bounds, fixed compact support and logarithmic derivative bounds. Blocked use: ['Applying the completed formula directly to an uncompleted sum without inversion.', 'Using the displayed remainder for weights with untracked derivative growth.', 'Discarding the dual coefficient support or its ramified factors.']
- **limit-type-i-level-and-height** (hypothesis): The low-height Type I comparison has a restricted outer length and logarithmic smoothness requirements. The height-average estimate instead requires derivative bounds independent of the asymptotic parameter and retains a decaying pole contribution. Blocked use: ['Extending the comparison beyond its stated level range.', 'Substituting rapidly varying inner weights into the height estimate without controlling their derivatives.', 'Ignoring the pole contribution before its height decay is sufficient.']
- **limit-structured-exceptional-family** (hypothesis): The exceptional-moment saving requires independently smooth prime convolutions, a squarefree product, bounded factor count and a fixed lower prime scale. Its extra twists, exclusions, norm height and conductor-length neighborhoods are restricted. Blocked use: ['Treating the exceptional estimate as an improvement for arbitrary divisor-bounded coefficients.', 'Introducing a sharp cutoff that couples prime factors.', 'Applying the saving outside the specified neighborhoods or height range.']
- **limit-existential-neighborhood-saving** (nonconstructive): The exceptional neighborhood and saving constants are obtained through a limiting-exponent contradiction and subsequence compactness, rather than an explicit finite parameter-selection procedure. Blocked use: ['Reporting explicit neighborhood radii or saving exponents that the source does not calculate.', 'Treating finite agreement as certification of the uniform exceptional-moment estimate.']
- **limit-full-sum-duality** (hypothesis): Smooth character duality applies to the complete primitive nonprincipal ideal sum. Missing Euler factors require explicit restoration or inclusion–exclusion, and conductor-dependent phases must remain outside the common polynomial coefficients. Blocked use: ['Applying the pole-free contour shift to a principal character.', 'Applying the primitive functional equation to an incomplete sum without accounting for omitted Euler factors.', 'Allowing row-dependent coefficient shifts inside a claimed common majorant.']
- **limit-radial-gram-weight** (hypothesis): The off-diagonal Gram estimate concerns primary squarefree character rows and a smooth radial norm weight with fixed compact support. Blocked use: ['Transferring the estimate to arbitrary feature vectors or arbitrary character families.', 'Replacing the radial weight with angularly varying weights without a new separation argument.']
- **limit-dispersion-regime-conditions** (hypothesis): Corrected low-height dispersion needs the sequence condition, roughness and its stated frequency-scale range. Height averaging has different height and derivative requirements; sharp norm-cell restrictions and unrestricted translated heights belong only to the stated smaller-length regime. Blocked use: ['Using corrected low-height variance without roughness or the sequence condition.', 'Transferring the freedom of sharp cell restrictions to the structured larger-length regime.', 'Applying a height-average diagonal estimate without its lower height threshold and derivative control.']
- **limit-cube-frequency-main-term** (edge_case): Nonzero cube frequencies produce the model contribution. They must be evaluated and matched with the mixed and model terms before estimating the corrected remainder. Blocked use: ['Discarding all nonzero Poisson frequencies as errors.', 'Assuming that the cube contribution vanishes after a nonzero angular twist.']
- **limit-exact-stopping-and-sparse-branches** (hypothesis): The decomposition identities operate on squarefree-supported kernels. Exact stopping separation requires the source bin predicate and reciprocal binomial weight; sparse late stops and the prime transition retain separate estimates. Blocked use: ['Replacing the bin predicate by a coupled least-prime cutoff.', 'Dropping the reciprocal selection weight.', 'Allowing added overlapping factors to contribute to a kernel that does not annihilate nonsquarefree products.', 'Treating every ordinary bilinear term as belonging to the corrected large-frequency-scale regime.']
- **limit-bilinear-height-localization** (hypothesis): The integrated bilinear estimate uses a smooth height cutoff, the stated shorter-length ranges and structural coefficient conditions. Its localization saving near the product boundary uses both norm variables. Blocked use: ['Replacing the smooth height average by an arbitrary pointwise height assertion.', 'Omitting localization on either coefficient side while retaining the same boundary saving.', 'Removing the structured convolution requirement at larger shorter-length scales.']
- **limit-fourier-boundary-control** (hypothesis): Fourier truncation requires the stated envelope and equal-norm coefficient bound. The aggregate endpoint error must remain in the sharp-cutoff assembly, including exact endpoint equality. Blocked use: ['Replacing the interval indicator by its truncated integral without a boundary error.', 'Applying the aggregate error estimate to coefficients whose equal-norm multiplicity is uncontrolled.']
- **limit-fixed-angular-parameter** (hypothesis): The angular integer is fixed before the norm cutoff tends to infinity, and subsequent constants may depend on that integer. Blocked use: ['Claiming uniform cancellation for angular modes growing with the norm cutoff.', 'Inferring estimates for progressively narrower angular sectors from pointwise fixed-mode cancellation.']
- **limit-twisted-sequence-condition** (hypothesis): The sequence condition must be proved for the angularly twisted coefficients themselves. Its verification here concerns the actual nonsparse ordinary decomposition coefficients. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the sequence condition survives every angular twist of an arbitrary sequence.', 'Invoking angular corrected dispersion from the untwisted sequence condition alone.']
- **limit-complementary-power-scope** (formal_scope): The fixed-power consequence covers exponents not divisible by three. Nonzero multiples of three reduce to prime-angle sums that the stated Hecke estimates do not control at the required stronger scale. Blocked use: ['Claiming resolution of every fixed-power case of the complementary conjecture.', 'Using ordinary angular prime cancellation as sufficient evidence for the stronger first-moment scale in the remaining cases.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 023 (`lean/docs/023.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An unconditional first moment for cubic Gauss sums](../../preprints/An-unconditional-first-moment-for-cubic-Gauss-sums-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Patterson's first-moment conjecture concerns the average of normalized cubic Gauss sums over primary Eisenstein primes. The formalization proves the sharp-cutoff asymptotic with main term $\frac65c_*X^{5/6}/\log X$, where $c_*=(2\pi)^{2/3}/(3\Gamma(2/3))$. It also proves the corresponding angular comparison for every integer Fourier mode and cancellation of order $o(X^{5/6}/\log X)$ for each fixed nonzero mode. All primary Eisenstein primes are included, without an extra conjectural premise.

Comparator source `PattersonFirstMoment.lean` (3246 bytes, sha256 `abe982565c44422f72daa2fbd2a28ef8ec180c19f9645bbf7400dbb668fbeb85`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Primary Eisenstein elements, residue characters, Gauss sums and first-moment target definitions. -/

section

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators
open Module
attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable

namespace CubicFirstMoment

def omega : ℂ := Complex.exp (2 * (Real.pi : ℂ) * Complex.I / 3)

def eisensteinRing : Subalgebra ℤ ℂ := Algebra.adjoin ℤ {omega}
abbrev Eisenstein := eisensteinRing

def omegaE : Eisenstein :=
  ⟨omega, Algebra.subset_adjoin (Set.mem_singleton omega)⟩

def norm (a : Eisenstein) : ℝ := Complex.normSq (a : ℂ)

def normNat (a : Eisenstein) : ℕ := ⌊norm a⌋₊

def primary (a : Eisenstein) : Prop := (3 : Eisenstein) ∣ a - 1

def primaryPrime (a : Eisenstein) : Prop := primary a ∧ Prime a

def modulus (a : Eisenstein) : Ideal Eisenstein := Ideal.span {a}

abbrev Residues (a : Eisenstein) := Eisenstein ⧸ modulus a

def residueRepresentative (a : Eisenstein) (v : Residues a) : Eisenstein :=
  Classical.choose (Ideal.Quotient.mk_surjective v)

def cubicSymbolAtPrime (p v : Eisenstein) : ℂ :=
  if IsUnit (Ideal.Quotient.mk (modulus p) v) then
    if h : ∃ j : Fin 3,
        Ideal.Quotient.mk (modulus p) (v ^ ((normNat p - 1) / 3)) =
          Ideal.Quotient.mk (modulus p) (omegaE ^ (j : ℕ)) then
      omega ^ ((Classical.choose h : Fin 3) : ℕ)
    else 0
  else 0

def additivePhase (p v : Eisenstein) : ℂ :=
  let z : ℂ := (v : ℂ) / (p : ℂ)
  Complex.exp (2 * (Real.pi : ℂ) * Complex.I * (z + star z))

def gaussAtPrime (p : Eisenstein) : ℂ :=
  (Real.sqrt (norm p) : ℂ)⁻¹ *
    ∑' v : Residues p,
      cubicSymbolAtPrime p (residueRepresentative p v) *
        additivePhase p (residueRepresentative p v)

def cStar : ℝ := (2 * Real.pi) ^ (2 / 3 : ℝ) / (3 * Real.Gamma (2 / 3))

def primeCutoffSum (f : Eisenstein → ℂ) (X : ℝ) : ℂ :=
  ∑' p : Eisenstein, if primaryPrime p ∧ norm p ≤ X then f p else 0

def firstMomentScale (X : ℝ) : ℝ := X ^ (5 / 6 : ℝ) / Real.log X

def FirstMomentStatement : Prop :=
  Asymptotics.IsLittleO Filter.atTop
    (fun X : ℝ => primeCutoffSum gaussAtPrime X -
      (((6 / 5 : ℝ) * cStar * firstMomentScale X : ℝ) : ℂ))
    firstMomentScale

def theta (ℓ : ℤ) (a : Eisenstein) : ℂ := ((a : ℂ) / (‖(a : ℂ)‖ : ℂ)) ^ ℓ

def AngularComparisonStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ ℓ : ℤ, Asymptotics.IsLittleO Filter.atTop
    (primeCutoffSum (fun p => theta ℓ p *
       (gaussAtPrime p - ((cStar * norm p ^ (-1 / 6 : ℝ) : ℝ) : ℂ))))
    firstMomentScale

def AngularCancellationStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ ℓ : ℤ, ℓ ≠ 0 → Asymptotics.IsLittleO Filter.atTop
    (primeCutoffSum (fun p => theta ℓ p * gaussAtPrime p)) firstMomentScale

/-- The sharp first moment over all primary Eisenstein primes. -/
theorem patterson_firstMoment : FirstMomentStatement := by
  sorry

/-- Model comparison in every fixed integer angular mode. -/
theorem patterson_angularComparison : AngularComparisonStatement := by
  sorry

/-- Cancellation at the first-moment scale for each nonzero angular mode. -/
theorem patterson_angularCancellation : AngularCancellationStatement := by
  sorry

end CubicFirstMoment
end
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Patterson's first-moment conjecture for normalized cubic Gauss sums over all primary Eisenstein primes, unconditionally. The sharp-cutoff main term is $`(6/5)c_*X^{5/6}/\log X`$, where $`c_*=(2\pi)^{2/3}/(3\Gamma(2/3))`$. For every fixed nonzero angular Fourier mode of the prime argument, we also prove cancellation at the first-moment scale.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:gauss-definition"></a>
Let $\omega=e^{2\pi i/3}$ and $\mathcal O=\mathbb Z[\omega]$. Write $N(z)=z\bar z=|z|^2$, $e(x)=e^{2\pi i x}$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(z)=z+\bar z$. An element prime to $3$ is called primary when it is congruent to $1$ modulo $3$. Every ideal prime to $3$ has exactly one primary generator. In particular, both conjugate prime ideals above a split rational prime are counted. For primary $b$, let $\chi_b$ be the cubic residue symbol with denominator $b$, extended by zero on nonunits modulo $b$, and put $$\label{eq:gauss-definition}
 G(b)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N(b)}}\sum_{v\bmod b}\chi_b(v)
           e\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(v/b)\bigr),
 \qquad c_* = \frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(2/3)}.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main"></a>
**Theorem 1** (First moment over primary primes). *As $X\longrightarrow\infty$, $$\label{eq:main}
 \sum_{\substack{N\pi\le X\\\pi\equiv1\pmod3\ \mathrm{prime}}}G(\pi)
 =\frac65c_*\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}
   +o\!\left(\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}\right).$$ The sum is over all primary primes in $\mathcal O$.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:angular">23</a> compares $G(\pi)$ with the elementary model $c_*N(\pi)^{-1/6}$ after inserting $(\pi/|\pi|)^\ell$, for every fixed integer $\ell$. For $\ell\ne0$ both the model and the twisted Gauss-sum moment are $o_\ell(X^{5/6}/\log X)$. The dependence on a fixed mode is essential; no uniformity in a growing angular parameter is asserted. The cubic Gauss-sum identity then gives cancellation at this same scale for every fixed power $G(\pi)^k$ with $3\nmid k$ and $|k|>1$; see <a href="#eq:gauss-power-cancellation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gauss-power-cancellation">[eq:gauss-power-cancellation]</a>.

Partial summation also gives the logarithmically weighted form <a id="eq:log-weighted-main"></a>$$\label{eq:log-weighted-main}
 \sum_{\substack{N\pi\le X\\\pi\equiv1\pmod3\ \mathrm{prime}}}
       G(\pi)\log N\pi
 =\frac65c_*X^{5/6}+o(X^{5/6}).$$ Indeed, if $A(y)$ denotes the sum in <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a>, then the weighted sum is $A(X)\log X-\int_2^X A(y)\,dy/y$; the integral is $O(X^{5/6}/\log X)$.

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is the asymptotic displayed after Equation (1.8) of Dunn–Radziwiłł . Equivalently, it is the rational-prime formulation stated by Wan <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Wan2021](#ref-Wan2021), Conjecture 4.13], as the conversion below shows. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves positively Patterson’s first-moment conjecture in these explicit normalizations. Patterson’s complementary conjecture predicts $o(X^{5/6}/\log X)$ for the sum of $G(\pi)^k$ for every fixed integer $k\notin\{0,\pm1\}$; see <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Equation (1.9)]. The fixed-power consequence above establishes the part with $3\nmid k$; nonzero multiples of three require stronger information about angular Hecke prime sums. The error here is little-oh at the first-moment scale. The distinction between the primary-prime and rational-prime normalizations is addressed below.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

For a rational prime $p\equiv1\pmod3$, Kummer’s sum is $$S_p=\sum_{x\bmod p}e(x^3/p).$$ Kummer’s numerical observations <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Kummer1846](#ref-Kummer1846)] suggested a bias toward positive values. Heath-Brown and Patterson <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HBP1979](#ref-HBP1979)] proved equidistribution of the normalized cubic Gauss sums on the unit circle. This controls the distribution on the prime-counting scale $X/\log X$, and is compatible with a smaller persistent first moment. Patterson <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Patterson1978](#ref-Patterson1978)] predicted a bias on the scale $X^{5/6}/\log X$. Heath-Brown’s cubic large sieve gave the unconditional upper bound $O_\epsilon(X^{5/6+\epsilon})$ <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HB2000](#ref-HB2000)]. His metaplectic mean-square estimate, recalled in Theorem <a href="#thm:hb-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:hb-height">7</a>, is also essential here: it controls Type I sums averaged over Mellin height.

The analytic continuation behind these estimates comes from cubic theta series. Within Kubota’s metaplectic theory, Patterson computed their coefficients and developed the relevant functional equation <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Patterson1977](#ref-Patterson1977)]; Yoshimoto studied character twists by related functional-equation methods <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Yoshimoto1987](#ref-Yoshimoto1987)]. Dunn and Radziwiłł extended these calculations to a level-aspect Voronoi formula <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Section 1.3 and Proposition 5.3]. Together with corrected dispersion, they proved a smoothed first-moment asymptotic under the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis for Hecke $L$-functions. The correction retains a main term from nonzero cube frequencies in Poisson summation before estimating the other frequencies. Their level formula itself is unconditional and is our exact imported input; their GRH-dependent cancellation estimates are not used.

The new cancellation needed here is confined to structured prime polynomials. We do not improve the cubic large sieve for arbitrary coefficients. Instead, moments at several lengths reduce a putative large contribution to a range in which a Gram-matrix estimate is strictly stronger. Two further points are important. An exact bin-based stopping rule gives independent coefficients without a coupled least-prime cutoff, and localization in both norm variables recovers enough diagonal saving at large Mellin heights to remove the smooth weight. These arguments may be useful in other first-moment problems where a structured dual family and a narrow product transition occur together.

<a id="sec:normalization"></a>
#### The rational-prime convention

<a id="eq:pairing"></a>
<a id="eq:rational-main"></a>
The exact conversion is $$\label{eq:pairing}
 G(\pi)+G(\bar\pi)=\frac{S_p}{\sqrt p},
 \qquad p=\pi\bar\pi\equiv1\pmod3.$$ Indeed cubic reciprocity and conjugation give $$\chi_\pi(\bar\pi)=\chi_{\bar\pi}(\pi)
     =\overline{\chi_\pi(\bar\pi)},$$ so this cube root of unity is $1$. Via the isomorphism $\mathbb Z/p\mathbb Z\simeq\mathcal O/(\pi)$, the additive coefficient in $e(\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(v/\pi))$ is $\bar\pi\bmod\pi$. It can therefore be removed without changing the cubic Gauss sum. Counting cube roots in $\mathbb F_p$ gives the sum of the two conjugate cubic Gauss sums, proving <a href="#eq:pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pairing">[eq:pairing]</a>. The conjugacy assertion also follows directly from the definition, since $\chi_\pi(-1)=1$. Inert primes have norm $p^2$ and contribute $O(\sqrt X)$ in total. Consequently Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives $$\label{eq:rational-main}
 \sum_{\substack{p\le X\\p\equiv1\pmod3}}
       \frac{S_p}{2\sqrt p}
\sim \frac35c_*\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}
 =\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{5\Gamma(2/3)}\frac{X^{5/6}}{\log X}.$$ This is precisely the coefficient in <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Wan2021](#ref-Wan2021), Conjecture 4.13]. That formulation sums over all rational primes; the primes $p\equiv2\pmod3$ have $S_p=0$ because cubing permutes $\mathbb F_p$, and $p=3$ does not affect the asymptotic.

<a id="stmt-rem-normalization"></a>
<div id="rem:normalization" class="remark">

*Remark 2* (Scope of the historical identification). The version of <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DR2024](#ref-DR2024)] cited here prints the coefficient $(6/5)c_*$ both in its rational-prime Conjecture 1, whose summand is $S_p/(2\sqrt p)$, and in its all-primary-prime display after Equation (1.8). These two displays cannot be equivalent under <a href="#eq:pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pairing">[eq:pairing]</a>. We use the latter display to identify the precise conjectural statement proved here; the rational consequence is <a href="#eq:rational-main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rational-main">[eq:rational-main]</a>. We do not silently identify the conflicting normalizations or claim to have settled which printed historical coefficient is authoritative.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The argument and its inputs

The analytic target is a comparison with the elementary model $c_*N(\pi)^{-1/6}$ on each dyadic prime interval. Its sum has the required constant by the prime ideal theorem and partial summation. The difficulty is to make that comparison with a sharp upper limit: smoothing the interval at a sufficiently fine scale introduces Mellin heights up to $X^{1/6+\rho}$ for a small fixed $\rho>0$.

At low heights, the level Voronoi formula gives the Type I comparison. For bilinear sums, Poisson summation has nonzero frequencies that are cubes. These frequencies produce precisely the model term, rather than an error. Corrected dispersion subtracts that term. The remaining frequencies are controlled by large sieves except in two conductor-length configurations. Section <a href="#sec:dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dual">4</a> treats these configurations by moments of several lengths and an off-diagonal Gram estimate. The coefficients there are independent smooth convolutions of prime sequences; this restriction is retained at every application.

An exact prime decomposition must therefore preserve coefficient independence. The stopping rule in Section <a href="#sec:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:decomposition">6</a> orders norm bins and counts the selected factors in the final bin. A binomial weight then separates the two sides exactly. This also isolates the only logarithmic transition, a product of three primes of nearly equal norm. Prime sparsity supplies enough saving there. At large heights the Gauss-sum term is treated without subtracting its model. Localizing *both* norm variables gives a smaller diagonal near the product boundary; away from that boundary, oscillation in height supplies the saving. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-map">1</a> records these joins.

<figure id="fig:proof-map">

<figcaption>The proof of the sharp first moment. Solid arrows indicate uses of proved estimates. The exact prime decomposition supplies the coefficient classes required by both bilinear branches.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:background" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:background">2</a> states the public arithmetic and analytic inputs, and Section <a href="#sec:typeI" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:typeI">3</a> derives the two Type I estimates. The structured moments of Section <a href="#sec:dual" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dual">4</a> enter the dispersion estimates of Section <a href="#sec:dispersion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dispersion">5</a>. The exact decomposition is proved in Section <a href="#sec:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:decomposition">6</a>; Section <a href="#sec:perron" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:perron">7</a> assembles all the estimates, fixes the order of parameters, and removes the smooth cutoff. Appendix <a href="#sec:angular" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:angular">8</a> proves the fixed-angular extension, including the shifted Hecke input, the angular Type I estimates and the required twisted sequence condition.

The formal literature inputs are cubic reciprocity and its supplementary laws; the functional equation, prime ideal theorem, and logarithmic conductor prime estimates for Hecke characters; Heath-Brown’s cubic large sieve and metaplectic height mean square; and the unconditional level Voronoi formula and residue of Dunn and Radziwiłł. Their normalizations and uniformity are stated where used. The ordinary character sieve, the nonsquarefree extensions of the cubic sieve, and all further cancellation and decomposition arguments are proved here. In particular, no GRH-dependent cancellation result is an input.

<a id="sec:background"></a>
### Arithmetic and analytic preliminaries

<span id="sec:preliminaries" label="sec:preliminaries"></span>

Write $K=\mathbb Q(\omega)$, $\mathcal O=\mathbb Z[\omega]$, and $\lambda=\sqrt{-3}$, where $\omega=e^{2\pi i/3}$. An element is *primary* if it is congruent to $1$ modulo $3$. Every nonzero ideal prime to $3$ has a unique primary generator. Unless specified otherwise, sums over $a,b,c,d,r,u$ and their divisors are over these generators. In contrast, Poisson frequencies range over the full lattice indicated in the formula. We use $$N(z)=|z|^2,\qquad \operatorname{Tr}(z)=z+\overline z,
 \qquad e(x)=\exp(2\pi i x).$$ The functions $\mu$, $\tau$, and $\varphi$ are the ideal Mobius, divisor, and Euler functions; thus $\varphi(r)=\#(\mathcal O/(r))^\times$. The ideal von Mangoldt function is $\Lambda(n)=\log N\pi$ when $n=\pi^k$ for a prime $\pi$ and $k\ge1$, and is zero otherwise. We write $\Omega(n)$ for the number of prime factors counted with multiplicity. The arithmetic function $\Lambda(n)$ is distinguished by its argument from a completed $L$-function $\Lambda(s,\chi)$.

For a primary prime $\pi$, the cubic residue symbol $\chi_\pi(v)$ is the element of $\{1,\omega,\omega^2\}$ congruent to $v^{(N\pi-1)/3}$ modulo $\pi$ when $(v,\pi)=1$, and is zero otherwise. Extend it multiplicatively in its lower argument. Put $$G(b)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{Nb}}\sum_{v\bmod b}
       \chi_b(v)e\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}(v/b)\bigr),
 \qquad c_* =\frac{(2\pi)^{2/3}}{3\Gamma(2/3)}.$$ Cubic reciprocity and its supplementary laws, in these conventions, give $\chi_b(a)=\chi_a(b)$ for primary $a,b$. The characters $b\mapsto\chi_b(\zeta\lambda^j)$, with $\zeta$ a unit, have bounded conductor supported above $3$ and trivial infinite parameter. We use these standard laws in the form recorded in <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DR2024](#ref-DR2024), Section 2].

<a id="eq:mult"></a>
Complete Gauss-sum evaluation and the Chinese remainder theorem give $$|G(a)|=\mu^2(a),\qquad
 G(ab)=G(a)G(b)\overline{\chi_b(a)}.
 \label{eq:mult}$$ For completeness, the additive character is primitive away from $3$. At a squarefree modulus the multiplicative character is primitive as well, so the unnormalized sum has absolute value $\sqrt{Na}$. At a repeated prime, summation over the final additive lift vanishes, whereas the multiplicative character is inflated from a smaller modulus. For coprime $a,b$, the two Chinese-remainder factors give $\chi_b(a)\chi_a(b)=\overline{\chi_b(a)}$ by reciprocity. If $(a,b)\ne1$, both sides of the second identity vanish.

For $h\ne0$ that is not a cube in $K$, the character $b\mapsto\chi_b(h)$ is nonprincipal. Indeed it is the cubic character of the nontrivial Kummer extension $K(\sqrt[3]{h})/K$. The splitting criterion at unramified primes, together with the prime ideal theorem for that extension, proves nontriviality. Passing to the associated primitive character or adding coprimality restrictions does not alter this assertion. Since $\mathcal O$ is integrally closed, an element of $\mathcal O$ that is a cube in $K$ is already a cube in $\mathcal O$.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Uniform conventions and classical inputs

Throughout the proof, $X$ tends to infinity and $L=\log X$. Lengths refer to norms. All dyads have fixed bounded endpoint ratios; bounded lengths are allowed. All variables and reciprocal scale parameters under consideration are bounded by fixed powers of $X$. A sequence is *divisor-bounded* if its absolute value is at most $L^C\tau(n)^C$ for some fixed $C$. Every fixed moment of this majorant on a norm dyad of length $B$ is $O(BL^{C'})$, with $C'$ fixed. The same assertion holds after collecting elements having the same rational norm: the number of ideals of norm $m$, and their divisor multiplicities, are bounded by fixed powers of the ordinary divisor function. These bounds follow from Euler products and the usual mean estimates for fixed divisor functions.

The constants in $\ll_\epsilon$ may depend on all fixed support, smoothness, and coefficient parameters. Each use of $X^\epsilon$ permits an arbitrarily small positive exponent, chosen after those parameters. We will explicitly retain logarithmic bounds when a small power loss would not suffice.

We use Mellin inversion with the convention $$\mathcal MW(s)=\int_0^\infty W(v)v^s\,\frac{dv}{v},\qquad
 W(v)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\sigma)}\mathcal MW(s)v^{-s}\,ds.$$ Smooth functions in products or ratios of norm variables can consequently be separated on fixed dyads. At fixed smoothness scale the Mellin integrands decrease faster than every power of their imaginary variables. A fixed number of derivatives bounded by powers of $L$ gives corresponding logarithmic costs. Derivative scales smaller than one will be tracked when they are introduced.

<a id="eq:hecke-sw"></a>
The classical analytic inputs are the functional equation for primitive Hecke characters, the prime ideal theorem and its Siegel–Walfisz form over the fixed field $K$, and the ordinary mean-value inequality for Dirichlet polynomials; see, for general analytic background, <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[IwaniecKowalski2004](#ref-IwaniecKowalski2004)]. For the precise finite-order completion, see <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Watkins2011](#ref-Watkins2011), Section 3.6], and for entireness see <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[ThornerZaman2019](#ref-ThornerZaman2019), Section 2C]. More precisely, if $\chi$ is primitive with trivial infinite parameter and conductor norm $D$, then $$\Lambda(s,\chi)
 =\left(\frac{\sqrt{3D}}{2\pi}\right)^s\Gamma(s)L(s,\chi),
 \qquad
 \Lambda(s,\chi)=\varepsilon_\chi\Lambda(1-s,\overline\chi),
 \quad |\varepsilon_\chi|=1.$$ The field has discriminant $-3$ and one complex place. The complex gamma factor in <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Watkins2011](#ref-Watkins2011)] is $2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)$; dividing the completion by the constant $2$ gives exactly this convention. For a nonprincipal character the completed function is entire. For every fixed $A,M>0$, nonprincipal cubic characters with primitive conductor norm at most $(\log x)^A$ satisfy, uniformly, $$\sum_{N\pi\le x}\chi(\pi)\ll_{A,M}x(\log x)^{-M}.
 \label{eq:hecke-sw}$$ Here is an explicit route to this uniformity. Apply <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ThornerZaman2019](#ref-ThornerZaman2019), Theorem 1.4] to the cyclic cubic extension of $K$ cut out by $\chi$. Its three characters are $1,\chi,\overline\chi$, and both nontrivial conductors have norm $D$. The parameters of that theorem are $n_K=2$, $D_K=3$, and $Q=D$. Weighting the three Frobenius-class counts by $\chi$ cancels their principal terms. The possible exceptional character is real; in a cyclic group of order three it must be trivial, so its term also cancels. The resulting error is at most a constant times $$\operatorname{Li}(x)\left\{
   \exp\!\left(-\frac{c\log x}{\log(12D)}\right)
       +\exp(-c\sqrt{\log x})\right\}+O(\log(2D)).$$ For $D\le(\log x)^A$, the theorem’s threshold $x\ge(12D)^{c_2}$ holds eventually, and this error gives <a href="#eq:hecke-sw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-sw">[eq:hecke-sw]</a> for every fixed $M$. The same theorem for a fixed extension gives the prime ideal theorem and the nonprincipality test used above. Standard partial summation gives the smooth and interval versions of <a href="#eq:hecke-sw" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hecke-sw">[eq:hecke-sw]</a>. Any additional omitted Euler factors will be accounted for through their deleted primes at the point of use. For coefficients indexed by rational integers we use the classical mean-value estimate of Montgomery and Vaughan <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[MontgomeryVaughan1974](#ref-MontgomeryVaughan1974)], <a id="eq:ordinary-mean-value"></a>$$\int_I\left|\sum_{n\le Z}v_n n^{it}\right|^2dt
 \ll (|I|+Z)\sum_{n\le Z}|v_n|^2,
 \label{eq:ordinary-mean-value}$$ where $I$ is any interval. An arbitrary fixed translation of the height is absorbed into the coefficients.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Two large sieves

<a id="stmt-thm-cls"></a>
<div id="thm:cls" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Heath-Brown’s cubic large sieve). *Let $A,B\ge1$, and let $(u_b)$ be supported on primary squarefree elements with $Nb\asymp B$. Then, for every $\epsilon>0$, $$\sum_{Na\asymp A}\mu^2(a)
 \left|\sum_b u_b\chi_a(b)\right|^2
 \ll_\epsilon (AB)^\epsilon
 \bigl(A+B+(AB)^{2/3}\bigr)\sum_b|u_b|^2.$$ The same assertion holds with upper cutoffs in place of dyads.*

</div>

This is <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[HB2000](#ref-HB2000), Theorem 2]. Its squarefree hypotheses apply to both arguments, and will not be discarded without the following explicit extensions.

The underlying planar large sieve is the number-field inequality associated with Huxley <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Huxley1968](#ref-Huxley1968)]; its exact $Z+Q^2$ scale is also recorded in <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[GaoZhao2022](#ref-GaoZhao2022), Equation (1.2)]. We give the primitive-character argument needed here, including the absence of a small-power conductor loss.

<a id="stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve"></a>
<div id="lem:ordinary-sieve" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (The ordinary character large sieve). *For distinct primitive ray characters of trivial infinite parameter, with conductor norm at most $Q$ and with conductor above $3$ dividing a fixed modulus, and coefficients supported on a norm dyad of length $Z$, one has $$\sum_\chi\left|\sum_n v_n\chi(n)\right|^2
 \ll (Z+Q^2)\sum_n|v_n|^2.$$ The implied constant is uniform when a fixed additional modulus above $3$ is allowed. In particular, the bound applies to the cubic characters indexed by coprime primary squarefree $p,q$ through $b\mapsto\chi_b(pq^2)$, whose conductor away from $3$ is $pq$. There is no $Q^\epsilon$ loss.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-ordinary-sieve</summary>

*Proof.* Fixing one of the finitely many classes at the bounded modulus above $3$ reduces the claim to primitive group characters modulo a primary $r$, with $Nr\ll Q$. Let $S(y)=\sum_n v_n e(\operatorname{Tr}(ny))$. Primitive Gauss-sum expansion and character orthogonality give $$\sum_{\chi\bmod r}^{\mathrm{primitive}}
       \left|\sum_n v_n\chi(n)\right|^2
 \le \frac{\varphi(r)}{Nr}
       \sum_{\substack{h\bmod r\\(h,r)=1}}|S(h/r)|^2
 \le \sum_{\substack{h\bmod r\\(h,r)=1}}|S(h/r)|^2.$$ Fixed factors arising from the trace-dual lattice are immaterial. Distinct reduced fractions with denominator norms $O(Q)$ are separated by $\gg Q^{-1}$ on the dual torus: their difference, after a lattice translation, has a nonzero integral numerator and denominator of absolute value $O(Q)$.

The planar additive large sieve for such a separated set has bound $O(Z+Q^2)$. Here is the usual smoothing argument. Take a nonnegative Schwartz majorant of the disk containing the support of $(v_n)$, at scale $\sqrt Z$. By duality and Poisson summation the resulting Gram matrix has entries bounded by $$C_N Z\sum_{\ell}(1+\sqrt Z\,|y-y'+\ell|)^{-N}.$$ Packing a $Q^{-1}$-separated set in successive planar annuli bounds each row sum by $O(Z+Q^2)$, for a fixed sufficiently large $N$. The operator norm has the same bound, proving the assertion. The exponents $1$ and $2$ at the primes dividing $pq$ distinguish the cubic characters in the last assertion; bounded ramified factors only introduce a bounded multiplicity. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-cubic-extensions"></a>
<div id="lem:cubic-extensions" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:inner-extension"></a>
<a id="eq:outer-extension"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Extensions of the cubic large sieve). *The following estimates allow an arbitrarily small $X^\epsilon$ loss. First, for divisor-bounded coefficients $(v_n)$ of norm length $Z$, without a squarefree restriction, $$\sum_{Na\asymp P}\mu^2(a)
       \left|\sum_n v_n\chi_a(n)\right|^2
 \ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon Z
       \bigl(P+Z+(PZ)^{2/3}\bigr).
 \label{eq:inner-extension}$$ Second, for coefficients $(u_b)$ on primary squarefree elements of norm length $P$, $$\sum_{Na\asymp J}\left|\sum_b u_b\chi_b(a)\right|^2
 \ll_\epsilon X^\epsilon
       \bigl(J+(JP)^{2/3}+J^{1/3}P\bigr)\sum_b|u_b|^2.
 \label{eq:outer-extension}$$ The latter also holds when $a$ ranges over all nonzero elements of $\mathcal O$ of norm $\asymp J$. If $J\gg P$, its last term is absorbed by $(JP)^{2/3}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-cubic-extensions"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-cubic-extensions</summary>

<a id="eq:inner-extension-dyad"></a>
*Proof.* For the first estimate write uniquely $n=s t^2 c^3$, with $s,t$ coprime and squarefree. Restrict their norms to dyads $S,V,C$, so that $SV^2C^3\asymp Z$. For each fixed $t,c$, the factors involving them have modulus at most one on the outer variable. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:cls" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cls">3</a> to the $s$ sum and then use the triangle inequality in the outer squared-sum norm. The squared norm of the restricted coefficient sequence is $O_\epsilon(X^\epsilon S)$. There are $O(VC)$ choices of $t,c$, and the resulting bound is $$X^\epsilon (VC)^2S
       \bigl(P+S+(PS)^{2/3}\bigr).
 \label{eq:inner-extension-dyad}$$ Since $(VC)^2S\ll Z$ and $S\ll Z$, summing the logarithmically many dyads proves <a href="#eq:inner-extension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:inner-extension">[eq:inner-extension]</a>. We retain <a href="#eq:inner-extension-dyad" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:inner-extension-dyad">[eq:inner-extension-dyad]</a> when a restriction makes $S$ shorter.

For the second estimate factor the outer argument in the same way.
